# Visual symbolism: sources gathered

This file is a public reference. It gathers what is written about how the nine grahas (planets), twelve rāśis (zodiac signs), five mahābhūtas (great elements) and three guṇas (strands of nature) look to the eye and the hand: colour, shape, size, line, texture, metal, gem, direction and feeling. It gathers; it proposes no assignment and draws no conclusion of its own.

Sanskrit is given in IAST, each term followed by its English. Where sources disagree, the readings stand side by side and each is named.

## How the source marks work

- **(read: …)** means the passage was opened and read; the mark names the text, the place in it and, where a part gives one, the address.
- **(memory)** means the statement comes from memory and no passage was in hand. It is not checked.
- **(summary only: …)** means the claim was seen only in a search summary of the page, which was not opened. It is unconfirmed.
- Some parts add a finer mark and state it where it first appears, such as "(read, summary: …)", a page opened through a summary rather than as raw text, and "(search only: …)", a claim seen in a search result alone.
- Each part also keeps its own list of what could not be found or opened.

## The four parts

- **Part One, The shastras.** What the Sanskrit astrological and related texts say: 1a the nine grahas, 1b the twelve rāśis, 1c the five mahābhūtas and three guṇas.
- **Part Two, Later authors.** 2a Western authors, from Plato to the twentieth century and into design theory; 2b Indian authors on altars, pedestals, architecture, rasa, Tantra and haṭha texts, and modern jyotiṣa.
- **Part Three, Measured or widely held outside astrology.** Studies of perception and psychology on the feeling of a colour, a shape, a line weight and a size. It is kept apart: it is not astrology and no part of it is tied to a graha.
- **Part Four, The table.** One table of the nine grahas by visible quality, collecting what Parts One and Two say, with nothing added.

## Part One: The shastras

### The nine grahas: what the Sanskrit astrological texts give to the eye and the hand

This part gathers statements from the texts. It proposes no assignments and draws no conclusions.

#### How to read this part

Sanskrit is given in IAST, each term followed by its English. The nine grahas (planets) are Sūrya (Sun), Candra (Moon), Maṅgala (Mars), Budha (Mercury), Guru or Bṛhaspati (Jupiter), Śukra (Venus), Śani (Saturn), Rāhu (the Moon's ascending node) and Ketu (the descending node).

Every statement carries a source mark.

- **(read: …)** means the passage was opened and read. The mark names the text, the chapter and verse, and a source code from the key below, which gives the URL.
- **(memory)** means the statement comes from memory or a secondary summary and was not read in a passage.

In the table at the end, a mark is shortened to a bracket such as `[BPHS 3.16]`, with the same codes. `[memory]` is memory.

Where the texts differ, the readings are set side by side and each is named. Differences between English translators of one Sanskrit verse are marked as differences of translation.

##### Source key

| Code | Text and edition opened | URL |
|---|---|---|
| BPHS-S | Bṛhat Pārāśara Horā Śāstra, Sanskrit text in Devanāgarī and IAST, with the site's own English gloss (the page names no translator). Chapter and verse as numbered on the site; chapter 3 numbering agrees with the English text BPHS-E below. | `https://enjoylearningsanskrit.com/scriptures/parashara/chapter-C/verse-N/` (C chapter, N verse) |
| BPHS-E | Bṛhat Pārāśara Horā Śāstra, English text of chapters 1–97, the archive.org item "Parashara Hora Sastra". The file names no translator; it carries notes marked "Addition from Santhanam" and a note in the first person referring to "my English Translation of Saravali", so it is in the voice of R. Santhanam. Chapter 3 "Grah Characters and Description"; chapter 32 "Karakatwas of the Grahas". | `https://archive.org/stream/ParasharaHoraSastra/BrihatParasharaHoraSastraVedicAstrologyEbook_djvu.txt` |
| BJ-S | Bṛhajjātaka of Varāhamihira, chapter 2 "Grahayonibhedādhyāya", Sanskrit text, verses 2.1–2.21, Wikisource. The text is an OCR-quality transcription with garbled letters; readings below are what the letters allow. | `https://sa.wikisource.org/wiki/बृहज्जातकम्/अध्यायः_२` |
| BJ-E | The Brihat Jataka of Varaha Mihira, English by N. Chidambaram Iyer (Foster Press, Madras, 1885), chapter II, stanzas 1–14. | `https://archive.org/details/brihatjatakavar00iyergoog` |
| SAR | Sārāvalī of Kalyāṇavarman, English text of chapters 1–10, archive.org item "Kalyana Varmas Saravali". The file names no translator. In the text opened, chapter 4 is "Planetary Characters" (verses 8, 12–16, 21–27), chapter 7 "Planetary Indications" (verses 7–13). The Sanskrit verses were not read. | `https://archive.org/details/KalyanaVarmasSaravali_201707` |
| PHD | Phaladīpikā of Mantreśvara, Sanskrit with English by V. Subrahmanya Sastri (Bangalore, 1937), adhyāya 2. The OCR prints some śloka numbers unclearly; numbers below are those printed or counted in order. | `https://archive.org/details/in.ernet.dli.2015.92117` |
| JP | Jātaka Pārijāta of Vaidyanātha, Sanskrit with English by V. Subrahmanya Sastri (1932), volume I, adhyāya II. OCR text; some śloka numbers are unclear. | `https://archive.org/details/JatakaParijata1932` |
| BS | Bṛhat Saṃhitā of Varāhamihira, English by N. Chidambaram Iyer (South Indian Press, Madura, 1884). Chapter numbers are this edition's (VI Mars, VII Mercury, VIII Jupiter, IX Venus, X Saturn, XVI "On the Planets"). OCR text. | `https://archive.org/details/bihatsahitvarah00iyergoog` |

#### Sections per graha

##### Sūrya (Sun)

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - raktaśyāma, reddish-dark (read: BPHS-S 3.16).
  - "blood-red" (read: BPHS-E 3.16–17); this and the line above translate the same verse differently.
  - raktaśyāma, "red and dark-brown" (read: BJ-S 2.4; BJ-E II.4).
  - tāmra, copper colour (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5).
  - copper-red (read: SAR 4.12–13); "a mix of red and green" (read: SAR 4.21).
  - "a dark-red form" (read: PHD 8); "a form with dark red rays" (read: JP 7); red (read: JP 19).
  - the Sun's rays "of the color of molten gold" (read: BS I.1, invocation).
  - the seasonal colours of the solar disc: copper or red-black in Śiśira, blue-crimson in Vasanta, slightly white and gold in Grīṣma, white in Varṣā, lotus-centre in Śarad, blood-colour in Hemanta (read: BS III.23–24).
  - garment: raktakṣauma, red silk (read: BPHS-S 3.43); coarse cloth (read: SAR 4.16; JP 22).
- **Eyes (dṛk, īkṣaṇa).** madhupiṅgala-dṛk, honey-tawny eyes (read: BPHS-S 3.23; BJ-S 2.8); "somewhat yellow" (read: BJ-E II.8); honey-coloured (read: SAR 4.21); reddish-brown (read: PHD 8).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** caturasra, square body (read: BPHS-S 3.23; BJ-S 2.8; SAR 4.21; PHD 8). BJ-E renders it "the height of the length of the two arms stretched out" (read: BJ-E II.8). "Massive arms" (read: PHD 8).
- **Size and proportion.** "not very tall" (read: SAR 4.21). BPHS 3.39 gives the Sun sthūla, stout-trunked trees (read: BPHS-E 3.39–40; the site's English in BPHS-S 3.39 reads the word as persons instead).
- **Texture (hair).** alpa-kaca, sparse hair (read: BPHS-S 3.23; BJ-S 2.8; PHD 8). SAR says "curly hair" (read: SAR 4.21), which differs.
- **Lustre (kānti, prabhā).** padma-patra-sama-dyuti, lustre like a lotus leaf or petal (read: BPHS-S 84.6); "fiery rays" and "prominent appearance" (read: SAR 4.21); Sūrya and Candra are "luminous" (read: JP 8).
- **Direction (diś).** East (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "the Eastern quarter" (read: PHD 15). Directional strength (digbala) is in the south (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** tāmra, copper (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12; SAR 4.16; PHD 30; JP 20). In BPHS 84.4 the verse lists copper first among the materials for images of the nine grahas, so copper stands at the Sun's place by position (read: BPHS-S 84.4). SAR chapter 7 says the Sun "rules … gold" (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** ruby (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** fire (read: PHD 27 text on constituents). BPHS 3.20 and BJ 2.6 list elements from Mars onward and give none for the Sun (read: BPHS-S 3.20; BJ-E II.6). BJ-E adds a translator's note, "the Sun is fire" (read: BJ-E II.6 note).
- **Temperament and feeling.** pitta-prakṛti, bilious constitution; śuci, pure; dhīmān, intelligent (read: BPHS-S 3.23). Sattva (read: BPHS-S 3.22; BJ-S 2.7; PHD 24). krūra, a malefic (read: BPHS-S 3.11). "Valiant and wrathful" (read: PHD 8); "courageous, steady … great and majestic" (read: SAR 4.21). Taste (rasa): kaṭu, pungent (read: BPHS-S 3.34). Body tissue: asthi, bone (read: BPHS-S 3.31).

##### Candra (Moon)

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - gaura-gātra, fair-bodied (read: BPHS-S 3.16); "tawny" in BPHS-E (read: BPHS-E 3.16–17), a difference of translation.
  - gaura (read: BJ-S 2.4); sita, white (read: BJ-S 2.5; SAR 4.12); "fair in complexion" (read: SAR 4.22).
  - "lean and white … beautiful in complexion" (read: PHD 9); "a youth with a white body" (read: JP 7).
  - in iconography, śveta, white, with white garments and white ornaments (read: BPHS-S 84.7); white silk garment (read: BPHS-S 3.43).
  - the Moon's disc at its good appearance is "white and of the color of the snow, of Kunda, of Kumuda and of crystal" and like "the stem of the lotus" (read: BS IV.30–31); ashy, red, rayless or broken discs are bad signs (read: BS IV.29).
- **Eyes and speech.** śubha-dṛk, auspicious look; madhu-vākya, sweet speech (read: BPHS-S 3.24); mṛdu-vāk, soft voice (read: BJ-S 2.8); "fine lovely eyes" (read: PHD 9).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** vṛtta-tanu, round body (read: BPHS-S 3.24). BJ gives tanu-vṛtta, "thin and round body" (read: BJ-S 2.8; BJ-E II.8). SAR: "emaciated body" (read: SAR 4.22). PHD: "huge body, young as well as old; lean" (read: PHD 9). JP 11 says the Moon "has the shape of a reptile", in a verse on the forms of the grahas (read: JP 11; the OCR is imperfect).
- **Size and proportion.** SAR 4.22 lists the Moon as "youthful, tall, curly and short hair" in one sentence (read: SAR 4.22); the translator's reading of which quality belongs to which noun is not clear.
- **Texture (hair).** "black and thin hair" (read: PHD 9); "curly and short hair" (read: SAR 4.22).
- **Lustre.** "luminous" (read: JP 8); the disc of a good Moon is "white" (read: BS IV.30–31).
- **Direction (diś).** North-west (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "North-west quarter" (read: PHD 15). Directional strength is in the north (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** The texts differ. maṇi, gems (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12; JP 20 "gems"); "gem (?)" with the translator's question mark (read: SAR 4.16); "bell-metal" (read: PHD 30); sphaṭika, crystal, for the image of the Moon (read: BPHS-S 84.4, paired by position). SAR chapter 7 says the Moon indicates "silver, conch, salt" (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** pearl (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** water (read: PHD 27). BJ-E has a translator's note "the Moon [is] water" (read: BJ-E II.6 note). BPHS 3.20 gives none (read: BPHS-S 3.20).
- **Temperament and feeling.** bahu-vāta-kapha, much wind and phlegm; prājña, learned; cañcala, fickle; madanātura, lovesick or lustful (read: BPHS-S 3.24). Sattva (read: BPHS-S 3.22). SAR: "soft, of Sattvik disposition, beautiful, phlegmatic, amicable to friends … kind … fickle-minded" (read: SAR 4.22). PHD: "wind and phlegm … mild in temperament" (read: PHD 9). Benefic when waxing, malefic when waning (read: BPHS-S 3.11, 3.37). Taste (rasa): kṣāra, saline (read: BPHS-S 3.34). Body tissue: rakta, blood (read: BPHS-S 3.31).

##### Maṅgala (Mars)

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - rakta, red (read: BPHS-S 3.16; BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5); "blood-red" (read: SAR 4.12–13, 4.23).
  - BJ 2.4 gives raktagaura, red-and-white, "both red and white" (read: BJ-S 2.4; BJ-E II.4), which differs.
  - "reddish hue" (read: PHD 10); "of a pale red colour" (read: JP 7); reddish (read: JP 19).
  - in iconography, red garlands and garments (read: BPHS-S 84.8); red garment (read: BPHS-S 3.44; PHD 10).
  - a favourable Mars shows "a large and clear disc or red like the flower of Kiṃśuka or of Aśoka or of clear and fine rays or like molten gold" (read: BS VI.14); enveloped in smoke or with a pointed flame is a bad sign (read: BS VI.11).
- **Eyes.** rakta-īkṣaṇa, red eyes (read: BPHS-S 3.25); "reddish-brown" (read: SAR 4.23); krūra-dṛk, cruel or sharp eyes (read: BJ-S 2.9; BJ-E II.9); "fierce eyes" (read: PHD 10).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** udāra-mūrti, noble or generous form (BPHS-E renders udāra as "liberal"); kṛśa-madhya-tanu, slender in the middle and in body (read: BPHS-S 3.25; BPHS-E 3.25). BJ: taruṇa-mūrti, young body; kṛśa-madhya, narrow middle (read: BJ-S 2.9; BJ-E II.9). "Strong body" (read: SAR 4.23). "Slender waist … youthful appearance" (read: PHD 10).
- **Size and proportion.** na-atyuccāṅga, not very tall (read: BPHS-S 3.16; BJ-S 2.4). SAR says "short" (read: SAR 4.23).
- **Thickness.** kṛśa-madhya, slender waist (read: BPHS-S 3.25; BJ-S 2.9).
- **Texture (hair).** "short and shining hair" (read: SAR 4.23); "curled and shining hair" (read: PHD 10).
- **Lustre.** "As bright as burning fire" (read: SAR 4.23).
- **Direction (diś).** South (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "the Southern quarter" (read: PHD 15). Directional strength is in the south (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** hema, gold (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12; JP 20 "gold"; SAR 4.16 "gold (?)"); "copper ore" (read: PHD 30); raktacandana, red sandalwood, for the image (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position). SAR chapter 7: "Mars rules blood, copper, Army, red cloth" (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** coral (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** agni, fire (read: BPHS-S 3.20; BJ-E II.6; PHD 27).
- **Temperament and feeling.** krūra, cruel; capala, fickle; krodhī, prone to anger; pitta-prakṛti (read: BPHS-S 3.25). Tamas (read: BPHS-S 3.22; BJ-S 2.7). SAR: "adventurous and wrathful and … skilful in hurting" (read: SAR 4.23). PHD: "wrathful but exceedingly generous" (read: PHD 10). Taste (rasa): tikta, bitter (read: BPHS-S 3.34). Body tissue: majjā, marrow (read: BPHS-S 3.31).

##### Budha (Mercury)

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - dūrvā-śyāma, dark like dūrvā grass (read: BPHS-S 3.16; BJ-S 2.4; BJ-E II.4 "green like the color of the bent grass").
  - harita, green (read: BJ-S 2.5; SAR 4.12); "complexion … of green grass" (read: SAR 4.24); "green as the blade of Durva" (read: PHD 11); "lovely dark green hue of Durva" (read: JP 7); "green" (read: JP 19).
  - in iconography, pīta garlands and garments and a radiance like the karṇikāra flower (read: BPHS-S 84.9), which differs from the green of BPHS 3.16. BPHS 3.44 gives him black silk (kṛṣṇakṣauma) (read: BPHS-S 3.44).
  - favourable colour at reappearance: "of the color of gold or of a parrot … or the hue of the Sasyaka gem", disc "glossy or big" (read: BS VII.20).
- **Eyes.** "reddish and broad" (read: SAR 4.24); "red and long eyes" (read: PHD 11).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** vapuḥ-śreṣṭha, excellent or attractive physique (read: BPHS-S 3.26). "Body … full of veins … even limbs" (read: PHD 11); "strong skin … sinewy" (read: SAR 4.24). JP 11 puts Budha with the Sun among those of the form of a bird (read: JP 11; OCR imperfect).
- **Size and proportion.** The texts read give none.
- **Texture.** "Strong skin" (read: SAR 4.24); tvak, skin, is his tissue (read: BPHS-S 3.31).
- **Lustre.** "Lovely" (read: JP 7); "glossy" favoured (read: BS VII.20); "moderate beauty" (read: SAR 4.24).
- **Direction (diś).** North (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "the Northern quarter" (read: PHD 15). Directional strength is in the east (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** yukti, mixture, which BJ-E renders "brass" (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12); "metal alloy" (read: SAR 4.16); "alloy of metals" (read: JP 20); "lead" (read: PHD 30); svarṇa, gold, shared with Guru (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position). SAR chapter 7: Mercury rules "minerals … gold" (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** emerald, "shaped like the bird Garuda" (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** bhūmi, earth (read: BPHS-S 3.20; BJ-E II.6; PHD 27).
- **Temperament and feeling.** śliṣṭa-vāk, which BPHS-E renders "capacity to use words with many meanings", BJ-E "an impediment in his speech" and SAR "clear in his speech"; atihāsya-ruci, very fond of jokes; a mixture of the three humours (read: BPHS-S 3.26; BPHS-E 3.26; BJ-E II.9; SAR 4.24). Rajas (read: BPHS-S 3.22). Benefic, but malefic when joined with a malefic (read: BPHS-S 3.11). SAR: "remains delighted … agreeable to all in dressing and speech" (read: SAR 4.24). Taste (rasa): miśra, mixed (read: BPHS-S 3.34).

##### Guru / Bṛhaspati (Jupiter)

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - gaura-gātra, fair-bodied (read: BPHS-S 3.17; BJ-S 2.4). BPHS-E renders the same verse "tawny" (read: BPHS-E 3.16–17). BJ-E renders BJ 2.4 "yellow like molten gold" (read: BJ-E II.4).
  - vyāpīta, very yellow (read: BJ-S 2.5, OCR); yellow (read: BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.12); "physical complexion … akin to pure yellow metal" (read: SAR 4.25); "yellowish hue" (read: PHD 12; JP 7); yellowish (read: JP 19).
  - in iconography, pīta, yellow (read: BPHS-S 84.10); a yellow garment pītāmbara (read: BPHS-S 3.43).
  - in omens, a good disc is "white jasmine or white water lily or crystal" (read: BS VIII.53); fire, yellow, dark-blue, green, blood or smoke-coloured discs are bad signs (read: BS VIII.17–18).
- **Eyes and hair.** piṅgala, tawny, in hair and eyes (read: BPHS-S 3.27; BJ-S 2.10); "yellow hairs and eyes" (read: BJ-E II.10); "somewhat reddish-brown" eyes (read: SAR 4.25); "brown" eyes and hair (read: PHD 12).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** bṛhad-gātra, big body (read: BPHS-S 3.27); bṛhat-tanu (read: BJ-S 2.10). SAR: "broad and prominent chest … firm and large eyes … fat" (read: SAR 4.25). PHD: "a fat and elevated chest … a big body" (read: PHD 12).
- **Size and proportion.** Big; BJ-E adds a translator's note that Jupiter is the biggest of the planets (read: BJ-E II.10 note).
- **Thickness.** "Fat" (read: SAR 4.25; PHD 12 "fat … chest"); vasā, fat, is his tissue (read: BPHS-S 3.31).
- **Texture.** The texts read give none beyond hair colour.
- **Voice.** "Resembles that of a lion" (read: SAR 4.25); "a lion or the sound of the conch" (read: PHD 12).
- **Direction (diś).** North-east (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "North-eastern quarter" (read: PHD 16). Directional strength is in the east (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** rajata, silver, in BJ (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12, with the translator's note that in his own house he governs gold); "silver (?)" (read: SAR 4.16); silver (read: JP 20); gold (read: PHD 30); svarṇa, gold, shared with Budha (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position). SAR chapter 7: "gold" (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** topaz (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** nabhas, ether (read: BPHS-S 3.20; BJ-E II.6 "akas"; PHD 27).
- **Temperament and feeling.** kapha-prakṛti; dhīmān, intelligent; sarva-śāstra-viśārada, expert in all the śāstras (read: BPHS-S 3.27). Sattva (read: BPHS-S 3.22). Benefic (read: BPHS-S 3.11). SAR: "learned in Vedas … firm … always fond of virtues and … modest … forgiving" (read: SAR 4.25). PHD: "exceedingly intelligent … always after wealth" (read: PHD 12). Taste (rasa): madhura, sweet (read: BPHS-S 3.34). BPHS 3.13: jñāna-sukha-da, giver of knowledge and happiness (read: BPHS-S 3.13).

##### Śukra (Venus)

- **Colour (varṇa).** The texts differ.
  - śyāva, dark brown (read: BPHS-S 3.17); "variegated" in BPHS-E (read: BPHS-E 3.16–17).
  - śyāma (read: BJ-S 2.4); BJ-E "neither very white nor very black" (read: BJ-E II.4).
  - citra, variegated (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.12; JP 19).
  - "a mix of yellow and green" (read: SAR 4.26).
  - "colour of the Durva sprout" (read: PHD 13); "a white person" (read: JP 7).
  - in iconography, śveta, white (read: BPHS-S 84.10).
  - a good disc is "of coagulated milk, of the white water lily, or of the moon" (read: BS IX.45); fire, blood, burnished-gold, green, ashy or black discs are bad signs (read: BS IX.44).
  - garments: kṣauma, silk (read: BPHS-S 3.43); "multi-coloured apparels" (read: SAR 4.26); "variegated colour" (read: PHD 13).
- **Eyes and hair.** su-locana, beautiful eyes (read: BPHS-S 3.28; BJ-S 2.10). vakra-mūrdhaja, curly hair (read: BPHS-S 3.28); BJ adds asita, black (read: BJ-S 2.10; BJ-E II.10). "Dark, short and extended hair … broad-eyed" (read: SAR 4.26); "black curled hair … broad eyes" (read: PHD 13).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** kānta-vapus, a charming or lustrous body (read: BPHS-S 3.28; BJ-S 2.10). SAR: "long hands, broad chest and face … prominent shoulders" (read: SAR 4.26). PHD: "limbs and body are huge" (read: PHD 13). JP 11 puts Śukra with Guru among bipeds (read: JP 11; OCR imperfect).
- **Size and proportion.** SAR "long hands, broad chest and face" (read: SAR 4.26); PHD "huge" (read: PHD 13).
- **Texture.** vakra, curly hair (as above). Body tissue: vīrya, semen (read: BPHS-S 3.31).
- **Lustre (kānti).** kānta-vapus (read: BPHS-S 3.28); SAR "splendourous" (read: SAR 4.26).
- **Direction (diś).** South-east (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "the South-eastern quarter" (read: PHD 16). Directional strength is in the north (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** muktā, pearls (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12; JP 20); "pearl (?)" (read: SAR 4.16); "silver" (read: PHD 30); rajata, silver, for the image (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position). SAR chapter 7 names diamonds and gold (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** diamond (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** jala, water (read: BPHS-S 3.20; BJ-E II.6; PHD 27).
- **Temperament and feeling.** sukhī, happy; kāvya-kartṛ, a poet; kapha-ādhikya, excess of phlegm; anilātman, windy (read: BPHS-S 3.28). Rajas (read: BPHS-S 3.22). SAR: "sensuous, windy and phlegmatic, very fortunate … sportive, intelligent" (read: SAR 4.26). PHD: "very lovely … treasured his virile power" (read: PHD 13). Benefic (read: BPHS-S 3.11). Taste (rasa): amla, sour (read: BPHS-S 3.34).

##### Śani (Saturn)

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - kṛṣṇa-deha, black body (read: BPHS-S 3.17; BJ-S 2.4); asita, black (read: BJ-S 2.5; SAR 4.12); "black-bodied" (read: SAR 4.27); "dark in form" (read: PHD 14; JP 7).
  - in iconography, indranīla-dyuti, the lustre of a sapphire (read: BPHS-S 84.11).
  - in omens, a good disc is "as bright as the cats'eye gem or pure or of the color of the bana (a black flower) or Atasi flower" (read: BS X.21); variegated, yellow, blood-coloured or ashy discs are bad signs (read: BS X.20).
  - garments: citra, variegated, and paṭṭa, fine silk (read: BPHS-S 3.44); black (read: SAR 4.27); a rag (read: PHD 30).
- **Eyes.** piṅga-dṛṣṭi, tawny (read: BPHS-S 3.29); kapila-dṛk, tawny or brown, which BJ-E renders "eyes of gold color" (read: BJ-S 2.11; BJ-E II.11); "red and depressed eyes" (read: SAR 4.27); "deep eyes" (read: PHD 14).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** kṛśa-dīrgha-tanu, lean and long body (read: BPHS-S 3.29; BJ-S 2.11). "Emaciated body, prominent veins" (read: SAR 4.27); "lean and tall body full of arteries and veins" (read: PHD 14). Paṅgu, lame (read: BPHS-S 3.29; PHD 14). JP 11 puts Śani with Maṅgala among quadrupeds (read: JP 11; OCR imperfect).
- **Size and proportion.** dīrgha, long or tall, and kṛśa, thin (read: BPHS-S 3.29).
- **Thickness.** sthūla-danta, large teeth (read: BPHS-S 3.29; BJ-S 2.11 sthūla-dvija); "large nails and teeth" (read: SAR 4.27; PHD 14).
- **Texture.** khara-roma-kaca, rough body-hair and head-hair (read: BPHS-S 3.29); paruṣa-roma-kaca, harsh hair (read: BJ-S 2.11); "stiff hair and limbs" (read: PHD 14); "very dirty" (read: SAR 4.27). Body tissue: snāyu, sinew (read: BPHS-S 3.31).
- **Lustre.** indranīla-dyuti (read: BPHS-S 84.11).
- **Direction (diś).** West (read: BJ-S 2.5; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); "Western quarter" (read: PHD 16). Directional strength is in the west (read: BPHS-S 3.35).
- **Metal (dhātu).** āyasī, iron (read: BJ-S 2.12; BJ-E II.12; SAR 4.16; PHD 30; JP 20); ayas, iron, for the image (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position). SAR chapter 7: "lead, zinc, black metals" (read: SAR 7.7–13).
- **Gem (ratna).** sapphire (read: PHD 29; JP 21).
- **Element (bhūta).** vāyu, air (read: BPHS-S 3.20; BJ-E II.6; PHD 27).
- **Temperament and feeling.** alasa, lazy; anilātmaka, windy (read: BPHS-S 3.29). duḥkha-da, giver of sorrow (read: BPHS-S 3.13). Tamas (read: BPHS-S 3.22). krūra, a malefic (read: BPHS-S 3.11). SAR: "talebearer … unkind, foolish … fierce, short-tempered and old" (read: SAR 4.27). PHD: "idle … exceedingly calumniating … cruel … no pity … dull-headed … fierce … a personification of anger" (read: PHD 14). Taste (rasa): kaṣāya, astringent (read: BPHS-S 3.34).

##### Rāhu

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - dhūmra-ākāra, smoky form, with nīla-tanu, a blue body (read: BPHS-S 3.30; BPHS-E 3.30 "smoky appearance with a blue mix physique").
  - in iconography, nīla-varṇa, blue, with a terrible face (karāla-vadana) (read: BPHS-S 84.12).
  - "black in colour" (read: PHD 33); "a body black in appearance" (read: JP 7).
  - the disc of Rāhu is black and invisible in the sky except at eclipses (read: BS V.2).
  - garments: citra-kanthā, a variegated or patched cloth (read: BPHS-S 3.42); "lead … and an old garment" (read: PHD 35).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** bhayaṅkara, terrifying; vana-stha, dwelling in forests (read: BPHS-S 3.30). In iconography, karāla-vadana with sword, shield and trident, mounted on a lion (read: BPHS-S 84.12). BS V.3 records opinions that he "resembles a serpent in shape with his head severed from his tail", or "is bodiless … mere darkness" (read: BS V.3). PHD: "tall in stature" (read: PHD 33).
- **Size and proportion.** "Tall in stature" (read: PHD 33).
- **Texture.** The texts read give none.
- **Direction (diś).** South-west (read: BJ-S 2.5 "tamas"; BJ-E II.5; SAR 4.8; JP 23); South-western direction for Rāhu and Ketu (read: PHD 16).
- **Metal (dhātu).** sīsa, lead (read: BPHS-S 3.42; PHD 35); sīsa for the image (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position). BPHS 3.47 lists Rāhu among the dhātu-grahas, those signifying minerals and metals (read: BPHS-S 3.47).
- **Gem (ratna).** BPHS 3.42 gives no gem for Rāhu (read: BPHS-S 3.42). PHD and JP give "agate" (read: PHD 29; JP 21); the Sanskrit name is gomeda (memory).
- **Element (bhūta).** None given for Rāhu in the passages read.
- **Temperament and feeling.** vāta-prakṛti, windy; dhīmān, intelligent; bhayaṅkara (read: BPHS-S 3.30). Krūra, a malefic (read: BPHS-S 3.11). JP 8: Rāhu and Ketu "are of the nature of darkness" (read: JP 8). PHD 33 differs: low caste, skin disease, a heretic, hiccough, "speaks falsehood … cunning … leprosy … reviles others and is devoid of intelligence" (read: PHD 33). Duration: eight months (read: BPHS-S 3.46).

##### Ketu

- **Colour (varṇa).**
  - tat-sama śikhī, "Ketu is akin to Rāhu" (read: BPHS-S 3.30), so smoky and blue-bodied by the verse's own comparison.
  - in iconography, dhūmra, smoky-coloured (read: BPHS-S 84.13).
  - "of a smoky colour" with "a red and fierce look" (read: PHD 34); "a variegated aspect" (read: JP 7).
  - garments: "a mud vessel and a mixed cloth of variegated colour" (read: PHD 35); chidra-yuta, holes or perforations, which BPHS-E renders "rags" (read: BPHS-S 3.42; BPHS-E 3.41–44).
- **Shape and outline (ākṛti, deha).** In iconography, dvi-bāhu, two-armed; gadin, mace-bearing; vikṛta-ānana, distorted faces; vulture seat (read: BPHS-S 84.13). PHD: "a venomous tongue, and an elevated body … armed … bruised limbs … lean" (read: PHD 34).
- **Size and proportion.** "Elevated body" (read: PHD 34); "lean" (read: PHD 34).
- **Texture.** The texts read give none.
- **Direction (diś).** South-west with Rāhu (read: PHD 16).
- **Metal (dhātu).** kāṃsya, bell metal, for the image (read: BPHS-S 84.4, by position); PHD 35 gives Ketu a mud vessel (read: PHD 35).
- **Gem (ratna).** nīla-maṇi, sapphire (read: BPHS-S 3.42). PHD and JP: "lapis lazuli or turkois" (read: PHD 29; JP 21); the Sanskrit name is vaidūrya (memory).
- **Element (bhūta).** None given in the passages read.
- **Temperament and feeling.** "Akin to Rāhu" (read: BPHS-S 3.30). Krūra (read: BPHS-S 3.11). JP 8: "of the nature of darkness" (read: JP 8). PHD: "fierce … malicious … outcast" (read: PHD 34). Duration: three months (read: BPHS-S 3.46).

#### What I could not find

- **Bṛhat Pārāśara Horā Śāstra, gems and karakatva.** The Sanskrit text I opened (chapters 3 and 84) gives for the grahas' gems only Ketu's sapphire (nīla-maṇi) in 3.42. No gem for Sūrya, Candra, Maṅgala, Budha, Guru, Śukra, Śani or Rāhu appears in the passages read. The gem lists come from Phaladīpikā and Jātaka Pārijāta. Chapter 32 "Karakatwas of the Grahas" in the English text opened treats the ātmakāraka and other variable significators and gives no colour or form; the grahas' fixed significations of this kind stand in chapter 3 verses 12–15. I did not read the other chapters of BPHS in full; I searched chapters 74–97 of the Sanskrit for gem names and found none.
- **Which English translator.** The BPHS Sanskrit site does not name its translator. The archive.org BPHS English file names none; it is in the voice of R. Santhanam by its own notes. A published Santhanam or Sharma volume was not opened as a printed edition. For Sārāvalī the file opened names no translator; the standard English rendering is Santhanam's (memory), but this was not confirmed from the file.
- **Sārāvalī in Sanskrit.** The verses were not read in Sanskrit; the Sanskrit scan at archive.org is too poorly recognised to read. Only the English text is the evidence for Sārāvalī. SAR marks "(?)" in its list of substances (gem, gold, silver, pearl); that doubt is the translator's.
- **Bṛhajjātaka in Bhaṭṭotpala's commentary, and in printed Sanskrit.** The Sanskrit I read is a Wikisource OCR-quality transcription, with garbled letters in some verses (notably 2.5 and 2.12). Pairings of 2.12's list of materials (tāmra, maṇi, hema, yukti, rajata, muktā, āyasī) with Sūrya through Śani follow Iyer's English, which agrees with the order of that list. Bhaṭṭotpala's commentary was not opened.
- **Sanskrit sites not reachable.** sanskritdocuments.org refused automated access (a challenge page), and the GRETIL address tried returned "not found". Jātaka Pārijāta, Phaladīpikā and Bṛhat Saṃhitā were therefore read only in Subrahmanya Sastri's and Chidambaram Iyer's English from archive.org, with the Sanskrit verses present on the scanned pages but not legible as text. The Sanskrit terms for Phaladīpikā, Jātaka Pārijāta and Bṛhat Saṃhitā in this file are therefore either the terms of the English translators' notes or marked (memory).
- **Bṛhat Saṃhitā.** The passages read give the colours of the planets as seen in good and bad omens (chapters III, IV, VI to X) and the shape opinions on Rāhu (V) and Ketu as comet (XI), and the "On the Planets" chapter (XVI) lists what each graha presides over. I found no passage giving each graha a fixed metal, gem or direction as the other texts do. The grahas' sizes and discs in the chapter on planetary conjunction (XVII) were read only in general terms (large, bright, glossy discs are favoured; small, dim ones are bad).
- **Elements of Sūrya and Candra, and of Rāhu and Ketu.** BPHS 3.20 and BJ 2.6 give elements from Maṅgala onward only. Phaladīpikā gives fire and water to Sūrya and Candra, but no element for Rāhu and Ketu.
- **Thickness, texture and lustre of several grahas.** For Candra, Budha, Rāhu and Ketu, the texts read give little or nothing under thickness and texture; Rāhu and Ketu have no lustre statement beyond colour and iconography.
- **Guru and Śukra colour.** The Sanskrit readings of Guru (gaura, fair) and Śukra (śyāva, dark brown) in BPHS 3.17 differ from the English text in which they are "tawny" and "variegated"; I could not decide between them, and the Sanskrit and English texts are both given in the table.
- **Memory items.** The Sanskrit gem names māṇikya, mukta, pravāla, marakata, puṣparāga, vajra, nīla, gomeda and vaidūrya are from memory, except nīla-maṇi in BPHS 3.42, which was read.

### The twelve rāśis (zodiac signs) as the Sanskrit astrological texts give them to the eye and the hand

This part collects what the texts say; it states no assignment of its own, draws no conclusion and sets no rule. Where the texts differ, the readings are laid side by side and each is named.

Sanskrit is in IAST, each term followed by its English. The twelve rāśis (zodiac signs) are Meṣa (Aries), Vṛṣabha (Taurus), Mithuna (Gemini), Karkaṭa (Cancer), Siṃha (Leo), Kanyā (Virgo), Tulā (Libra), Vṛścika (Scorpio), Dhanus (Sagittarius), Makara (Capricorn), Kumbha (Aquarius), Mīna (Pisces).

#### How the source marks work

- `(read: TEXT chapter.verse [KEY])` means I opened that passage. `[KEY]` stands for the URL listed under "Sources opened" below. The verse number is the one in the edition I opened; editions number differently.
- `(memory)` means the statement comes from memory or a secondary summary, not from a passage I opened.
- Most of the texts were opened as OCR text of scanned books. OCR errors are possible, and where a figure or word looked damaged I say so.

#### Sources opened

Bṛhat Parāśara Horā Śāstra (BPHS), chapter 4, "Zodiacal Signs Described", verses 1–24:

- **[S]** English by R. Santhanam, *Brihat Parasara Hora Sastra of Maharshi Parasara*, vol. 1 (Ranjan Publications, New Delhi), chapter 4, pp. 48–52 of the book, verse ranges as he gives them (6–7, 8, 9–9½, 10–11, 12, 13–14, 15–16½, 17–18½, 19–20, 21–21½, 22–24). https://archive.org/download/BPHSEnglish/BPHS%20-%201%20RSanthanam.pdf (OCR of the printed book).
- **[S2]** A second e-text of the same English wording for chapter 4, whose file gives no translator's name; read as a cross-check, it agrees with [S]. http://vedic-astro.s3.amazonaws.com/books/bhrihat_parasara_hora_shastra.pdf
- **[E]** The Sanskrit of chapter 4 in Devanagari and IAST, verses 1–30 (the Aries to Pisces descriptions are verses 6–24). The edition is not named on the site. I read the Sanskrit; I did not use the automatically made English printed beside it. https://enjoylearningsanskrit.com/scriptures/parashara/chapter-4

Bṛhajjātaka of Varāhamihira, chapter 1, "Zodiac Signs", verses 4, 5, 8, 10, 11, 19, 20:

- **[BJ]** Sanskrit text of chapter 1, verses 1–20, from the Wisdomlib edition (Sanskrit text and English by Michael D. Neely; only the Sanskrit appeared in what I fetched). https://www.wisdomlib.org/hinduism/book/brihat-jataka-by-varahamihira-sanskrit-english/d/doc1501536.html
- **[BJ-V]** English with word-for-word notes, titled on the archive as "Brihat Jataka of Varahamihira by Swami Vijnananda" (the printed title page reads "alias Hari Prasanna Chatterjee"). https://archive.org/stream/BrihatJatakaOfVarahamihiraBySwamiVijnananda/Brihat%20Jataka%20of%20Varahamihira%20By%20Swami%20Vijnananda_djvu.txt
- **[BJ-Sa]** English by V. Subrahmanya Sastri, 2nd edition, with notes. https://archive.org/stream/BrihatJataka2ndEd.ByVSubrahmanyaSastri/Brihat%20Jataka%202nd%20Ed.%20by%20V%20Subrahmanya%20Sastri_djvu.txt

Sārāvalī of Kalyāṇavarman:

- **[SR]** English by R. Santhanam, *Saravali of Kalyana Varma*, vol. 1 (Ranjan Publications), chapter 3, "Rasi Description" (Rāśi-prabheda), verses 3–41. https://static1.squarespace.com/static/5e5b12e392faf542399f9528/t/6444ef10e62dac013a8d316a/1682239298930/Saravali+-+R+Santhanam+-+Vol-1.pdf
- **[SR2]** A second English e-text of the same work's chapter 3, which agrees with [SR]; used only for the index and for verses 3–22. https://archive.org/stream/KalyanaVarmasSaravali_201707/Kalyana+Varmas+Saravali_djvu.txt

Phaladīpikā of Mantreśvara:

- **[PD-Sa]** English by V. Subrahmanya Sastri, 2nd edition, 1950: Adhyāya I (slokas 4–9) and Adhyāya IX (slokas 1–13). https://archive.org/stream/Phaladeepika2ndEd.1950ByVSubrahmanyaSastri/Phaladeepika%202nd%20Ed.%201950%20by%20V%20Subrahmanya%20Sastri_djvu.txt

Verse numbers for Sārāvalī, Phaladīpikā, Jātakapārijāta and Bṛhat Saṃhitā are the translators' sloka numbers.

#### Qualities the texts give to all twelve alike, with the pattern each text states

These patterns are stated once here; each rāśi's section then applies them.

- **Quality of the sign (cara, sthira, dvisvabhāva; movable, fixed, dual)**: from Meṣa in cycle (read: BPHS 4.5, "carasthiradvisvabhāvāḥ" [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]; SR 3.20–21 [SR]; JP I.13 [JP]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Cruel or mild (krūra, saumya)**: alternating from Meṣa, which is krūra (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V], "cruel, wicked, impulsive, malefic, militant" and "mild, benefic, pleasing, gentle" are the translator's glosses of krūra and saumya; JP I.13 [JP]; PD I.9, "fierce" and "auspicious" [PD-Sa]).
- **Sex (puruṣa, strī; male, female), odd and even (viṣama, sama)**: alternating from Meṣa, which is male and odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]; SR 3.20–21, table of odd/even and male/female [SR]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Direction (diś)**: Meṣa east, Vṛṣabha south, Mithuna west, Karkaṭa north, and the other signs of each "triangle" repeat the same direction. (read: BJ 1.11, "with their trikoṇas" [BJ-V]; SR 3.22 [SR]; JP I.13 [JP]; PD I.9, table [PD-K].) BPHS 4.6–22 names the direction for eleven of the signs and is silent for Karkaṭa (read: [S], [E]).
- **Kālapuruṣa limb (the sign as a part of the body of Time personified)**, listed from Meṣa to Mīna:
  - BPHS 4.4: head, face, arms, heart, stomach, hip, space below navel, privities, thighs, knees, ankles, feet (read: [S]).
  - BJ 1.4: head, face, neck with upper chest and shoulders, heart, stomach, navel, intestines (the translator's gloss of the word printed as the seventh), private parts, thighs, joints, calves, feet (read: [BJ-Sa]).
  - SR 3.5–6: head, face, arms, heart, stomach, hip, space below navel and private parts, private parts, thighs, knees, ankles, feet (read: [SR]).
  - JP I.8: head, mouth, breast, heart, belly, hip, groins, private part, two thighs, two knees, two calves, two feet (read: [JP]).
- **The figure the sign is named for (ākṛti)** for the signs the texts draw: see the section of each sign. BJ 1.5 in Sanskrit: "matsyau ghaṭī nṛmithunaṃ sagadaṃ savīṇaṃ cāpī naro 'śvajaghano makaro mṛgāsyaḥ / taulī sasasyadahanā plavagā ca kanyā śeṣāḥ svanāmasadṛśāḥ svacarāśca sarve" (read: [BJ]). The remaining signs "correspond to things with properties indicated by their respective names" (read: BJ 1.5, Sastri's English [BJ-Sa]).
- **Number of feet (pada)** as the texts group the signs. Dvipada (biped, human signs): Mithuna, Kanyā, Tulā, Kumbha, the first half of Dhanus. Catuṣpada (quadruped): Meṣa, Vṛṣabha, Siṃha, the second half of Dhanus, the first half of Makara. Jalacara (watery): Karkaṭa, Mīna, the second half of Makara. Kīṭa (reptile or insect, centipede): Vṛścika. (read: BJ 1.5 notes [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.22–23 notes [SR]; PD I.7 [PD-Sa]; JP I.16–18 [JP].) BPHS describes Karkaṭa as "many-footed, a centipede sign" and Vṛścika the same (read: BPHS 4.10–11, 4.15–16 [S]); SR's note says "Parasara includes Cancer also" among the kīṭa signs (read: SR 3.22 notes [SR]).

#### Per rāśi

##### 1. Meṣa (Aries; the ram)

- **Colour (varṇa)**: rakta, "blood-red" (read: BPHS 4.6, "raktavarṇaḥ" [E], [S]). Rakta, "blood red" (read: BJ 1.20 [BJ-V]); "red" (read: BJ 1.20 [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.40–41 [SR]; JP I.23 [JP]).
- **Figure (ākṛti)**: a ram, "identical with its name" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.3–4 [SR]; JP I.9 [JP]). Its place: JP gives "the surface of the earth containing precious stones and yielding minerals" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); PD gives "forest" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); BPHS gives "wanders in hills", śailacārī (read: BPHS 4.7 [E]).
- **Size and proportion**: bṛhadgātra, "large-bodied", "prominent physique" (read: BPHS 4.6 [E], [S]). Length of the sign as the texts rank it: hrasva, short (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38, "short ascension" [SR]).
- **Feet**: catuṣpad, quadruped (read: BPHS 4.6 [E]).
- **Direction (diś)**: east, pūrvavāsī (read: BPHS 4.7 [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]; SR 3.22 [SR]).
- **Element (bhūta)**: pāvakī, fiery (read: BPHS 4.7 [E]).
- **Quality**: cara, movable (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour (the text's temperament-type)**: pitta, bilious (read: BPHS 4.5, "Aries, Leo and Sagittarius are bilious" [S]).
- **Rising**: pṛṣṭhodaya, rising with the back (read: BPHS 4.7 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]; PD I.8 [PD-Sa]).
- **Day or night**: rātrivikramī, strong at night (read: BPHS 4.6 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; JP I.14 [JP]). "Blind at the dead of night" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: male, odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Temperament, mood, disposition**: rajoguṇī, of the guṇa (quality of nature) rajas (passion, great activity); krūra, fierce; Santhanam adds "It denotes courage" and "related to kings" (read: BPHS 4.6–7 [S], [E]). Nature of the person born with it rising, PD IX.1: round eyes, weak knees, fierce (Sastri), "aggressive temperament" (Kapoor), afraid of water, eats sparingly, passionate or longing for women, always on his legs or moving, fickle (Sastri), speaks falsehoods, bruised limbs (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Meṣa: eats sparingly, longs for women, enterprising elder brothers (read: JP IX.91 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet (as the texts class the sign by water)**: "traverses waterless regions" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: dhātu, mineral (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). Substances it presides over, BS XLI.2: cloths, sheep's wool, clothes of goats' hair, lentils, wheat, resin, barley, herbs growing on land, gold (read: [BS]).

##### 2. Vṛṣabha (Taurus; the bull)

- **Colour**: śveta, white (read: BPHS 4.8 [E], [S]; BJ 1.20 [BJ-V], [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.40–41 [SR]; JP I.23 [JP]).
- **Figure**: a bull, "identical with its name" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]). Place: "table-land, farms, the cow station and the forest" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "agricultural land with water" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]).
- **Size and proportion**: dīrgha, long (read: BPHS 4.8 [E], [S]). Opposite reading: hrasva, short (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38, "short ascension" [SR]). The first speaks of the sign's body, the second two of its length as a rising sign (JP's note on I.13 calls it "length"; SR's heading calls it "ascension"); I make no choice between them.
- **Feet**: catuṣpad, quadruped (read: BPHS 4.8 [E]).
- **Direction**: south, yāmya (read: BPHS 4.8 [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: "an earthy sign" (read: BPHS 4.8 [S]; the Sanskrit line ends "vaṇigbhūmirajaḥ pṛṣṭhodayo vṛṣaḥ" [E], a compound I do not resolve).
- **Quality**: sthira, fixed (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: vāta (anila), windy (read: BPHS 4.5, "Taurus, Virgo and Capricorn are windy" [S]).
- **Rising**: pṛṣṭhodaya, with the back (read: BPHS 4.8 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]; PD I.8 [PD-Sa]).
- **Day or night**: śarvarībalī, strong at night (read: BPHS 4.8 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; JP I.14 [JP]). "Blind at the dead of night" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: female, even; saumya, mild (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Temperament**: BPHS names its people, not its mood: villages and businessmen (read: BPHS 4.8 [S]). PD IX.2, the person with it rising: plump thighs, big face, agricultural, happy in the middle and last parts of life, fond of young women, liberal in gifts (Sastri) or of sacrificing and forgiving nature (Kapoor), endures hardship, owns cattle, marks or moles on the back, face and sides (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Vṛṣabha: liberal, lovely, rich in fame, excellent behaviour (read: JP IX.91 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: "water-resorter" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: mūla, vegetable (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.3: cloths, flowers, wheat, rice, barley, buffaloes and bullocks (read: [BS]).

##### 3. Mithuna (Gemini; the pair)

- **Colour**: BPHS 4.9–10: haridvarṇa, "green (grass-like) hue" (read: [E], [S]). BJ 1.20 gives the third colour as "śukatanunibha", the parrot's body: "yellow, of the colour of the parrot" (read: [BJ-V]) against "green (parrot's hue)" (read: [BJ-Sa]); SR 3.40–41: "green" (read: [SR]); JP I.23: "green (parrot's hue)" (read: [JP]).
- **Figure**: nṛmithuna, a pair, man and woman, "sagadaṃ savīṇaṃ", with mace and lute (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ]; BPHS 4.9 [E]; SR 3.3–4, "veena and mace" [SR]; JP I.9 [JP]). Place: "gambling house and pleasure haunts such as a park or a garden" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "bedroom" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]).
- **Size and proportion**: samagātra, "even body", well-proportioned (read: BPHS 4.10 [E], [S]). Rising length: "even", medium (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38, "medium ascension" [SR]).
- **Feet**: dvipad, biped (read: BPHS 4.9 [E]).
- **Direction**: west, pratyag (read: BPHS 4.9 [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: vāyu, airy; anilī, windy (read: BPHS 4.9 [E], [S]).
- **Quality**: dvisvabhāva, dual (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: mixed (read: BPHS 4.5, "Gemini, Libra and Aquarius are mixed" [S]).
- **Rising**: śīrṣodaya, with the head (read: BPHS 4.9 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]). Phaladīpikā puts Mithuna in the ubhayodaya (both) class together with Mīna (read: PD I.8 [PD-Sa]; Kapoor's English lists "Gemini and Pisces" [PD-K]). Sastri's note to BJ 1.10 reads: "According to some, Mithuna is also an Ubhayodaya sign" (read: [BJ-Sa]).
- **Day or night**: rātribalī, strong at night (read: BPHS 4.9 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; JP I.14 [JP]). "Blind at midday" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: male, odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: "windy in temperament", "lives in villages" (read: BPHS 4.9 [S]); Brahmin, forest-dweller (read: BPHS 4.10 [E]). PD IX.3, the person with it rising: black eyes, curly hair, enjoys women, thick neck (Kapoor), many friends (Kapoor), reads others' thoughts, elevated nose, fond of music and dancing, keeps at home (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Mithuna: long life, skilled in love, fond of jest and merriment (read: JP IX.91 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: "water-resorter" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: jīva, animal (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.3: corn, autumnal creepers such as vines, bulbs of lilies, cotton (read: [BS]).

##### 4. Karkaṭa (Cancer; the crab)

- **Colour**: pāṭala, "pale red" (read: BPHS 4.10 [E]; 4.10–11 [S]). BJ 1.20: pāṭala, "pink, pale red, of the colour of the pāṭalī (trumpet) flower" (read: [BJ-V]); "pink" (read: [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.40–41 [SR]; JP I.23 [JP]).
- **Figure**: a crab, "identical with its name" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]). Place: "a lake, a pond or a sand bank in the midst of water" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "a chasm with water in it" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); BPHS: forests (read: [S]).
- **Size and proportion**: sthaulyatanu, "bulky body" (read: BPHS 4.11 [E], [S]). Rising length: "even", medium (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38 [SR]).
- **Feet**: bahupādacara, "many feet", a centipede sign (read: BPHS 4.10–11 [S], [E]). Sastri's note to BJ 1.5: "Some say that Aquarius is a watery Sign and Cancer is a centiped sign" (read: [BJ-Sa]).
- **Direction**: north (read: BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]; SR 3.22 [SR]; PD I.9 [PD-K]). BPHS gives no direction for Karkaṭa (read: [S], [E]).
- **Element**: jalī, watery (read: BPHS 4.11 [E], [S]).
- **Quality**: cara, movable (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: śleṣma, phlegmatic ("the rest are phlegmatic") (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: pṛṣṭhodaya (read: BPHS 4.11 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]; PD I.8 [PD-Sa]).
- **Day or night**: strong at night (read: BPHS 4.10–11 [S]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; JP I.14 [JP]). "Blind at midday" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: female, even; saumya, mild (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Temperament**: sattvaguṇī, "Sattvic in disposition (seen in gods)" (read: BPHS 4.10–11 [S], [E]); Brahmin (read: BPHS 4.10–11 [S]). PD IX.4, the person with it rising: henpecked, thick or fleshy neck, many friends, many houses, rich, thick waist (Kapoor) or elevated buttocks (Sastri), short, crooked (Kapoor) or crooked in his views (Sastri), fond of water sports, walks fast, few sons (read: [PD-K], [PD-Sa]). The Moon in Karkaṭa: heart impassioned with love, a wandering life, eloquent speech (read: JP IX.91 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: jalacara, "watery" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: dhātu, mineral (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.4: paspalum, plantains, dūrvā grass, fruits, bulbs, fragrant leaves, coconuts (read: [BS]).

##### 5. Siṃha (Leo; the lion)

- **Colour**: BPHS 4.12: pāṇḍu, "pale"; Santhanam renders "a large, white body" (read: [E], [S]). BJ 1.20 reads the fifth colour as "dhūmrapāṇḍu": "smoky" (read: [BJ-V]), against "pale white" (read: [BJ-Sa]; JP I.23 [JP]). SR 3.40–41 gives the fifth colour as "brown" (read: [SR]).
- **Figure**: a lion, "identical with its name" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]). Place: "deep mountain caves and dense forests" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "mountain" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); "forest-dwelling", vanī (read: BPHS 4.12 [E]).
- **Size and proportion**: bṛhadgātra, "large-bodied" (read: BPHS 4.12 [E]; "a large, white body" [S]). Rising length: dīrgha, long (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38, "long ascension" [SR]).
- **Feet**: catuṣpād, quadruped (read: BPHS 4.12 [E]).
- **Direction**: east (read: BPHS 4.12 [E]; PD I.9 [PD-K]).
- **Element**: BPHS 4.12 states none (read: [E], [S]). Memory: other presentations class it as fire (memory).
- **Quality**: sthira, fixed (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: pitta, bilious (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: śīrṣodaya (read: BPHS 4.12 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]).
- **Day or night**: dyuvīryavān, strong in the day (read: BPHS 4.12 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]). "Blind at the dead of night" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: male, odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: sattvī, Sattvic; a "royal sign", kṣatriya (read: BPHS 4.12 [E], [S]); krūra, fierce (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]). PD IX.5, the person with it rising: reddish eyes, large chin, broad face, arrogant, powerful, angry at trifles, fond of forests and hills, obedient to his mother, firm-minded (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Siṃha: broad eyes, beautiful face, grave looks, happy (read: JP IX.92 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: "waterless regions" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: mūla, vegetable (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.4: husk-grains, juices, skins of lions, jaggery (read: [BS]).

##### 6. Kanyā (Virgo; the maiden)

- **Colour**: citra, variegated (read: BPHS 4.14, "citravarṇā" [E], [S]; BJ 1.20 [BJ-V]; JP I.23, "piebald" [JP]). SR 3.40–41: the sixth colour is "grey" (read: [SR]).
- **Figure**: a woman in a boat, with corn and fire: "sasasyadahanā plavagā ca kanyā" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ]); "a woman sailing in a boat with corn in one hand and fire in the other" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]); "grains and fire in its hands" (read: BPHS 4.13–14 [S]); "a virgin sitting in a boat with a lamp in her hands" (read: SR 3.3–4 [SR]). Place: "a pasture land or the pleasure room of a woman" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "land with water and corn" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); hill-resorting, pārvatīya (read: BPHS 4.13 [E]).
- **Size and proportion**: madhyāṅgā, "of medium build" (read: BPHS 4.13 [E], [S]). Rising length: dīrgha, long (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38 [SR]).
- **Feet**: dvipād, biped (read: BPHS 4.13 [E]).
- **Direction**: south, yāmya (read: BPHS 4.13 [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: BPHS states none (read: [E], [S]).
- **Quality**: dvisvabhāva, dual (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: vāta, windy (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]). BPHS 4.14 also says "prabhañjinī", which Santhanam renders "relates to hurricanes" (read: [E], [S]).
- **Rising**: śīrṣodaya (read: BPHS 4.13 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]).
- **Day or night**: dinabalānvitā, strong in the day (read: BPHS 4.13 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]). "Blind at midday" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: female, even; saumya, mild (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Temperament**: "kumārī tamasā yuktā bālabhāvā", a virgin, joined with tamas (the dark, heavy guṇa), of a child's nature (read: BPHS 4.14 [E]; Santhanam: "a Virgin and is Tamasa (a disposition of demons)" [S]); vaiśya, trade (read: BPHS 4.14 [E]). PD IX.6, the person with it rising: truthful, kind in speech, a look faint with modesty (Sastri) or eyes full of bashfulness (Kapoor), shoulders and arms drooping, versed in the śāstras, few sons (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Kanyā: graceful address, eminent in learning, prosperous (read: JP IX.92 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: "water-resorter" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: jīva, animal (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.5: common flax, kalāya corn, horse-gram, wheat, green gram, leguminous seeds (read: [BS]).

##### 7. Tulā (Libra; the balance)

- **Colour**: kṛṣṇa, black (read: BPHS 4.15 [E]; "black in complexion" [S]; BJ 1.20 [BJ-V], [BJ-Sa]; JP I.23 [JP]). SR 3.40–41 lists "variegated" in the seventh place and "black" in the eighth (read: [SR]).
- **Figure**: taulī, a man (naraḥ) with scales in hand (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ], [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.3–4, "a male holding Scales in hand" [SR]; JP I.9 [JP]). Place: "the bazaar of a city abounding in all kinds of valuable things" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "the house of a Vaisya" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); BPHS: "resorts to land" (read: [S]).
- **Size and proportion**: madhyatanu, "medium build" (read: BPHS 4.15 [E], [S]). Rising length: dīrgha, long (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38 [SR]).
- **Feet**: dvipād, biped (read: BPHS 4.15 [E]).
- **Direction**: west, paścima (read: BPHS 4.15 [E]; BJ 1.11 [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: bhūcara, land-going (read: BPHS 4.15 [E]).
- **Quality**: cara, movable (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: mixed (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: śīrṣodaya (read: BPHS 4.15 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]).
- **Day or night**: dyuvīryāḍhya, strong in the day (read: BPHS 4.15 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]). "Deaf in the forenoon" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: male, odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: rajoguṇī (of rajas); ghātī, "destructive or mischievous" (read: BPHS 4.15–16 [E], [S]); śūdra (read: [S]). PD IX.7, the person with it rising: lean and frail, tall, reveres gods and Brahmins, deft in trading, brave, merciless, impartial in argument, wandering, two names, few children (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Tulā: reverence to gods and Brahmins, affection to relatives, opulent (read: JP IX.92 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: waterless regions (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: dhātu, mineral (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.5: black gram, barley, wheat, mustard (read: [BS]).

##### 8. Vṛścika (Scorpio; the scorpion)

- **Colour**: piśaṅga, "reddish-brown" (read: BPHS 4.16 [E]; 4.15–16 [S]). BJ 1.20 gives the eighth colour as "kanakasadṛśa", "golden" (read: [BJ-V], [BJ-Sa]; JP I.23 [JP]). SR 3.40–41 gives the eighth colour as "black" (read: [SR]).
- **Figure**: a scorpion, "identical with its name"; also called Kīṭa (insect) and Kaurpi (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]; JP I.5–7 [JP]). Place: "a region abounding in flint, or holes of noxious reptiles" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "a hole or cavity" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); bilī, hole-dweller (read: BPHS 4.16 [E]).
- **Size and proportion**: BPHS 4.16–17: svalpāṅga, "small-bodied", Santhanam "slender physique"; "romasvāḍhya", rich in hair, "hairy physique"; "atitīkṣṇāgra", very sharp at the point, Santhanam "very sharp (or passionate)" (read: [E], [S]). Rising length: dīrgha, long (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38 [SR]).
- **Feet**: bahupād, many-footed; kīṭa, centipede or insect sign (read: BPHS 4.15–16 [S], [E]; PD I.7 [PD-Sa]).
- **Direction**: north (read: BPHS 4.15–16 [S], "saumyastha" [E]; BJ 1.11, from the pattern of Karkaṭa's triangle [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: jalabhūvaḥ, "water and land" (read: BPHS 4.16 [E]; "resorts to water and land" [S]).
- **Quality**: sthira, fixed (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: śleṣma, phlegmatic (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: śīrṣodaya (read: BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]). BPHS: Santhanam gives no rising for Vṛścika; the Sanskrit prints "pṛṣṭhodayī tvatha dhanuḥ" at the join of Vṛścika and Dhanus, a word that may belong to either (read: BPHS 4.17 [E]); I do not resolve it.
- **Day or night**: dinavīryāḍhya, strong in the day (read: BPHS 4.16 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]). "Deaf in the forenoon" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: female, even (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: "very sharp (or passionate)" (read: BPHS 4.16 [S]); Brahmin; saumya (read: [E]). PD IX.8, the person with it rising: round thighs and knees, broad chest and wide eyes, ill from an early age, separated from parents and teachers, does cruel acts, honoured by the king, lotus marks on the feet (Kapoor) or on hands and feet (Sastri) (read: [PD-K], [PD-Sa]). The Moon in Vṛścika: heedless, ailing, avaricious, wandering (read: JP IX.92 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: watery, jalacara (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: mūla, vegetable (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.6: sugar-cane, whatever grows by watering, iron or bell-metal, goats' and sheep's wool (read: [BS]).

##### 9. Dhanus (Sagittarius; the bow)

- **Colour**: piṅgala, "tawny" (read: BPHS 4.18 [E], [S]; BJ 1.20 [BJ-V]). "Yellowish or brown" (read: BJ 1.20 [BJ-Sa]; JP I.23 [JP]). SR 3.40–41 gives the ninth colour as "golden" (read: [SR]).
- **Figure**: a man armed with a bow, "cāpī naro 'śvajaghanaḥ", the lower body that of a horse (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ], [BJ-Sa]; BPHS 4.17–18, "dhanurdhara", "adores an arch" [E], [S]; SR 3.3–4 [SR]). Place: "a king's residence" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); "the camp or barrack containing horses, chariots and elephants" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "moves on earth", vasudhācārī (read: BPHS 4.19 [E]).
- **Size and proportion**: samagātra, "even build" (read: BPHS 4.18 [E], [S]). Rising length: "even", medium (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38 [SR]).
- **Feet**: dvipād in its first half and catuṣpād in its second: "ādāvante catuṣpādaḥ" is printed in the Sanskrit (read: BPHS 4.18 [E]; [S]: "biped in first half, second half quadruped").
- **Direction**: east, pūrvastha (read: BPHS 4.19 [E]; BJ 1.11, pattern [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: pāvaka, fiery (read: BPHS 4.18 [E], [S]).
- **Lustre**: tejasvī, "lustrous, splendorous" (read: BPHS 4.19 [E], [S]).
- **Quality**: dvisvabhāva, dual (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: pitta, bilious (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: pṛṣṭhodaya (read: BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]; PD I.8 [PD-Sa]). BPHS, Santhanam's English: "rises with its head" (read: BPHS 4.17–18 [S]); the Sanskrit as printed has "pṛṣṭhodayī" at BPHS 4.17 [E] (see the note under Vṛścika).
- **Day or night**: niśivīryāḍhya, strong at night (read: BPHS 4.18 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; JP I.14 [JP]). "Deaf in the afternoon" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: male, odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: sāttvika, Sattvic; kṣatriya; royal (read: BPHS 4.17–18 [E], [S]); krūra, fierce (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]). PD IX.9, the person with it rising: very long face and neck, big nose and ears, always at some business, eloquent, dwarfish (Sastri) or short (Kapoor), courageous, wealthy, a favourite of the king, to be won by kindness (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Dhanus: handsome limbs, bright eyes (read: JP IX.93 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: waterless regions (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: jīva, animal (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.6: horses, salt, cloths, missiles, sesamum, corn, roots (read: [BS]).

##### 10. Makara (Capricorn; the sea-creature with a deer's face)

- **Colour**: karbura, "variegated" (read: BPHS 4.20 [E]; 4.19–20 [S]). BJ 1.20: karbura, "gray" (read: [BJ-V]) against "variegated" (read: [BJ-Sa]; JP I.23 [JP]). SR 3.40–41 gives the tenth colour as "yellow" (read: [SR]). (Memory: karbura is given in dictionaries as variegated, spotted or tawny; (memory).)
- **Figure**: a makara (crocodile) with a deer's face, "makaro mṛgāsyaḥ" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ], [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.3–4, "Deer-faced sign" [SR]); BPHS: first half quadruped, second half footless and moving in water (read: BPHS 4.19–20 [S]). Place: "a river or other water-abounding region" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "watery forests" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); "forests and lands", vanabhūcara (read: BPHS 4.20 [E]).
- **Size and proportion**: bṛhadgātra, "large body" (read: BPHS 4.20 [E], [S]). Rising length: "even", medium (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38 [SR]).
- **Feet**: first half catuṣpad, second half vipada (footless) and jalaga (water-going) (read: BPHS 4.20 [E]).
- **Direction**: south (read: BPHS 4.19 [E]; BJ 1.11, pattern [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: bhaumī, earthy (read: BPHS 4.19 [E]; "an earthy sign" [S]).
- **Quality**: cara, movable (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: vāta, windy (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: pṛṣṭhodaya (read: BPHS 4.20 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]).
- **Day or night**: niśi vīryavān, strong at night (read: BPHS 4.19 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; JP I.14 [JP]). "Deaf in the afternoon" (read: JP I.21 [JP]).
- **Sex**: female, even (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: tamī, of tamas, "predominance of Tamoguna (a disposition seen in demons)" (read: BPHS 4.19 [E], [S]). PD IX.10, the person with it rising: weak lower limbs, vitality (Kapoor) or exceeding strength (Sastri), lazy, a religious hypocrite, fond of wandering, shameless, wind diseases (read: [PD-K], [PD-Sa]). The Moon in Makara: acquainted with music, broad-headed, addicted to women not his own (read: JP IX.93 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: jalacara, watery (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: dhātu, mineral (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.7: trees, shrubs, whatever grows by watering, sugar-cane, gold and "black metal" (iron) (read: [BS]).

##### 11. Kumbha (Aquarius; the water-pot)

- **Colour**: babhru, "brown", "deep-brown" (read: BPHS 4.21 [E]; 4.21–21½ [S]). BJ 1.20: babhru, "of the colour of the body of a mongoose" (read: [BJ-V]); "deep brown" (read: [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.40–41 [SR]; JP I.23 [JP]).
- **Figure**: a man with a pot, "ghaṭī nṛ" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ]); "kumbhī naraḥ" (read: BPHS 4.21 [E]); "a man holding a pot" (read: SR 3.3–4 [SR]); "a man with a pot" (read: JP I.9 [JP]). Place: "the spot frequented by potters" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); "a water-pot and the receptacles of vessels in a house" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]); "resorts to deep water", jalamadhyastha (read: BPHS 4.21 [E], [S]).
- **Size and proportion**: madhyatanu, "medium build" (read: BPHS 4.21 [E], [S]). Rising length: hrasva, short (read: JP I.13 [JP]; SR 3.37–38, "short ascension", Aquarius [SR]).
- **Feet**: dvipād, biped (read: BPHS 4.21 [E]).
- **Direction**: west (read: BPHS 4.21–22 [S]; BJ 1.11, pattern [BJ-V]).
- **Element**: vāta, "airy" (read: BPHS 4.21 [E], [S]).
- **Quality**: sthira, fixed (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: mixed (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: śīrṣodaya (read: BPHS 4.21 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]).
- **Day or night**: dyuvīrya, "very strong in daytime" (read: BPHS 4.21 [E], [S]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]).
- **Sex**: male, odd (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Temperament**: tamas, "Tamasic"; śūdra (read: BPHS 4.21–22 [E], [S]); krūra, fierce (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]). PD IX.11, the person with it rising: does sinful deeds secretly, a body like a water-pot, covetous, enduring long marches, fond of perfumes and flowers, gains and losses of money (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). The Moon in Kumbha: without virtue or morality, inimical to learned men while surpassing in knowledge (read: JP IX.93 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: "water-resorter" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: mūla, vegetable (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.7: water-products, fruits, flowers, gems and "brilliant things" (read: [BS]).

##### 12. Mīna (Pisces; the two fishes)

- **Colour**: BPHS gives Mīna no colour (read: BPHS 4.22–24 [S], [E]). BJ 1.20: svaccha, "of the colour of the body of clear white fishes", "sparkling white" (read: [BJ-V]); "white" (read: [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.40–41 [SR]; JP I.23 [JP]).
- **Figure**: matsyau, two fishes, "one tailed with the head of the other" (read: BPHS 4.22–24 [S]); "mīnau pucchāsyasaṃlagnau", fishes joined at tail and mouth (read: BPHS 4.22 [E]); "two fishes with their two tails and heads in reversed positions" (read: BJ 1.5 [BJ-Sa]; SR 3.3–4, "a pair of fish" [SR]; JP I.9 [JP]). Place: "water" (read: PD I.5 [PD-Sa]); "a river, the sea and similar collections of water" (read: JP I.10–12 [JP]).
- **Size and proportion**: madhyadehī, "medium build" (read: BPHS 4.23 [E], [S]). Rising length: JP I.13 puts it with the "even" signs, and its own note on that sloka says the measure of I.50 would make it short; SR 3.37–38 lists Mīna as "short ascension" (read: [JP], [SR]); Santhanam notes "In Jataka Parijata, Ch. I, Pisces is stated to be of medium ascension" (read: SR 3.37–38 notes [SR]).
- **Feet**: apada, footless (read: BPHS 4.23 [E]); jalacara, water-going (read: BPHS 4.22–24 [S]).
- **Svaccha, clarity (lustre)**: svaccha means clear; the same word is the colour word of BJ 1.20 (read: [BJ-V], "clear white fishes"). PD IX.12: "a symmetrical and shining body" (Sastri) and "beautiful and symmetrical" (Kapoor) (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]).
- **Direction**: north, saumyastha (read: BPHS 4.23 [E]; "rules the north" [S]).
- **Element**: jalī, watery (read: BPHS 4.23 [E], [S]).
- **Quality**: dvisvabhāva, dual (read: BPHS 4.5 [E]).
- **Humour**: śleṣma, phlegmatic (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]).
- **Rising**: ubhayodaya, with both head and back (read: BPHS 4.23 [E]; BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]; PD I.8 [PD-Sa]).
- **Day or night**: the texts differ.
  - BPHS 4.22 in the Sanskrit as printed: "mīnarāśirdivābalī", strong in the day (read: [E]). Santhanam's English of the same verses: "night-strong" (read: [S]).
  - BJ 1.10 puts it among the day signs (read: [BJ-V], by exclusion from the night list); SR 3.24 lists Pisces among the day signs (read: [SR]); PD I.8 gives the nocturnal signs as the back-risers and Mithuna, so Mīna is diurnal (read: [PD-Sa]).
- **Sex**: female, even; saumya, mild (read: BPHS 4.5 [S]; PD I.9 [PD-Sa]).
- **Temperament**: sattvaguṇāḍhya, rich in sattva (the light, clear guṇa); svastha, "healthy"; Santhanam: "denotes resoluteness" (read: BPHS 4.23 [E], [S]). PD IX.12: learned, grateful, satisfied with his wife and fortune; Sastri adds drinks water excessively (read: [PD-K], [PD-Sa]). The Moon in Mīna: a handsome person, learned, lord of many wives (read: JP IX.93 [JP]).
- **Dry or wet**: jalacara, "watery" (read: JP I.15 [JP]).
- **Class of substance**: jīva, animal (read: PD I.9 [PD-Sa]; JP I.19 [JP]). BS XLI.8: jewels from testaceous animals such as pearl oysters, water-products, diamonds, various oils, fish-products (read: [BS]).

#### Where the texts disagree, side by side

**Colour of each sign, as each text lists the twelve in order**

| Sign | BPHS 4.6–23 [E][S] | BJ 1.20 [BJ-V] | BJ 1.20 [BJ-Sa] | SR 3.40–41 [SR] | JP I.23 [JP] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Meṣa | rakta, blood-red | blood red | red | red | red |
| Vṛṣabha | śveta, white | white | white | white | white |
| Mithuna | haridvarṇa, green | yellow, parrot | green, parrot | green | green, parrot |
| Karkaṭa | pāṭala, pale red | pink | pink | pink | pink |
| Siṃha | pāṇḍu, pale / white | smoky | pale white | brown | pale white |
| Kanyā | citra, variegated | variegated | piebald | grey | piebald |
| Tulā | kṛṣṇa, black | black | black | variegated | black |
| Vṛścika | piśaṅga, reddish-brown | golden | golden | black | golden |
| Dhanus | piṅgala, tawny | tawny | yellowish or brown | golden | yellowish or brown |
| Makara | karbura, variegated | gray | variegated | yellow | variegated |
| Kumbha | babhru, deep brown | mongoose colour | deep brown | deep brown | deep brown |
| Mīna | none given | clear white fish | white | white | white |

Each cell is as that translator renders it. The Sārāvalī sequence, as Santhanam's English gives it, runs red, white, green, pink, brown, grey, variegated, black, golden, yellow, deep brown, white (read: SR 3.40–41 [SR]); I have not opened its Sanskrit, so I cannot say whether the shift from the Bṛhajjātaka order lies in the text or in the rendering.

**Rising by head or back**

- Pṛṣṭhodaya (back) signs: Meṣa, Vṛṣabha, Karkaṭa, Dhanus, Makara (read: BJ 1.10 [BJ-V]; SR 3.24 [SR]; JP I.14 [JP]; PD I.8, Sastri's English [PD-Sa]). Kapoor's English of PD I.8 lists "Taurus, Cancer, Aries and Capricorn" in the first sentence and "Taurus, Cancer, Sagittarius, Aries and Capricorn" in the second (read: [PD-K]).
- Mithuna: śīrṣodaya in BJ 1.10, SR 3.24, JP I.14 and BPHS 4.9 (read: [BJ-V], [SR], [JP], [E]); ubhayodaya in PD I.8 (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]); BJ's note records "according to some" ubhayodaya (read: [BJ-Sa]).
- Dhanus: pṛṣṭhodaya in BJ, SR, JP and PD; BPHS in Santhanam's English: "rises with its head" (read: BPHS 4.17–18 [S]); in the Sanskrit as printed, "pṛṣṭhodayī" stands at 4.17 between Vṛścika and Dhanus (read: [E]).
- Mīna: ubhayodaya in all texts that speak.

**Day or night strength of Mīna**: see section 12 above.

**Rising length (long, medium, short) by sign**

| Text | Short (hrasva) | Medium (sama, madhya) | Long (dīrgha) |
|---|---|---|---|
| JP I.13 [JP] | Meṣa, Vṛṣabha, Kumbha | Makara, Mithuna, Dhanus, Mīna, Karkaṭa | Vṛścika, Kanyā, Siṃha, Tulā |
| SR 3.37–38 [SR] | Mīna, Vṛṣabha, Meṣa, Kumbha | Mithuna, Dhanus, Karkaṭa, Makara | Vṛścika, Kanyā, Siṃha, Tulā |

The two differ only in Mīna (read: [JP], [SR]). JP I.50 gives the rising measures of the first six signs as 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40 and says the same figures in the inverse order give the second six (read: [JP]; the first and fifth figures are damaged in the OCR, and I read them from the pattern and from the note that multiplies them by 10 to give 200, 240 and so on).

**Body length versus rising length of Vṛṣabha**: BPHS 4.8 says "dīrgha", long (read: [E], [S]); JP I.13 and SR 3.37–38 say short (read: [JP], [SR]). See the note under Vṛṣabha.

**Water classes of the signs**: JP I.15 gives three, "watery": Mīna, Vṛścika, Karkaṭa, Makara; "water-resorters": Kumbha, Kanyā, Mithuna, Vṛṣabha; "waterless regions": Meṣa, Dhanus, Tulā, Siṃha (read: [JP]). PD I.7 and SR 3.22–23 notes give Karkaṭa, Mīna and the second half of Makara as watery, with Vṛścika the kīṭa (read: [PD-Sa], [SR]).

**Height-and-build words in BPHS and the ascendant chapter of Phaladīpikā**: BPHS 4.17–18 says Dhanus is "even build" (read: [S]); PD IX.9 says the person with Dhanus rising is "dwarfish" (Sastri) or "short" (Kapoor) (read: [PD-Sa], [PD-K]). BPHS 4.15 says Tulā is medium build (read: [S]); PD IX.7 says the person with Tulā rising is tall and lean (read: [PD-Sa]). These speak of the sign and of the person born with it rising; I set them side by side without joining them.

#### What I could not find

- **Heat and cold (uṣṇa, śīta) of the signs**: no text I opened speaks of a sign as hot or cold. The nearest quality is the humour (pitta, vāta, mixed, śleṣma) of BPHS 4.5, listed in the column "Humour". The column "Heat / cold" is therefore empty for all twelve signs.
- **Dry and wet as one scheme**: only Jātakapārijāta I.15 sorts the signs by water (watery, water-resorting, waterless). I found no text giving each sign a dry or moist quality in those words.
- **Thickness and texture (sthūla, kṛśa as thickness; smooth, rough)** beyond the size words already given: no text I read speaks of the thickness or texture of a sign itself. The few touch and sight words that appear are Vṛścika's "romasvāḍhya" (hairy) and Dhanus's "tejasvī" (lustrous) in BPHS, Mīna's "svaccha" (clear) in BJ 1.20, and the substances of Bṛhat Saṃhitā XLI (wool, flax, gems, iron and so on).
- **Element (bhūta) of Siṃha, Kanyā and Tulā**: BPHS 4.12–16 states none for Siṃha or Kanyā, and "bhūcara" (land-going) for Tulā. No other text I read gives the four-element class (fire, earth, air, water) to all twelve; Bṛhat Saṃhitā gives none. Memory only: the common scheme of three signs to an element exists in other presentations, which I did not open (memory).
- **Direction of Karkaṭa in BPHS**: BPHS 4.10–11 gives none (read: [S], [E]); it comes from BJ 1.11, SR 3.22 and PD I.9.
- **Colour of Mīna in BPHS**: none given (read: [S], [E]).
- **Bṛhat Saṃhitā**: it gives the signs neither colour, figure, size, direction nor temperament in the passages I opened. I found only the classes of people affected by an eclipse in each sign (Adhyāya V, slokas 35–42, "if eclipsed in Vṛṣabha: shepherds, cattle..."; read: [BS]) and the substances each sign presides over (Adhyāya XLI, slokas 2–8). I searched the Sastri translation for sign names. I downloaded the Chidambaram Iyer and Ramakrishna Bhat translations but did not read them: my search of their OCR for the sign names returned nothing, which may be the OCR.
- **Sārāvalī**:
  - The Sanskrit of chapter 3 was not opened in script. The Santhanam OCR is garbled in the verse lines, and the English of verse 23 (the strength of the human, quadruped and watery signs by direction) is missing from the OCR page; I took that matter from Santhanam's notes under verse 22.
  - I did not find in chapter 3 or elsewhere in volume 1 a rāśi-by-rāśi description of the sort BPHS 4 gives; the sign descriptions there are the figures (verses 3–4), the colours (40–41), the classes (20–24) and the ascension lengths (37–38).
  - A second-volume file at the same host returned the same file as volume 1; volume 2 was not opened.
  - The chapters on the Sun, Moon, Mars and other planets in each sign (chapters 22 and following in volume 1) were not read for sign temperament.
- **Bṛhajjātaka**: only chapter 1 was read. The Wisdomlib page I fetched showed the Sanskrit and not the English or word-for-word analysis, so the English readings are Vijnananda's and Sastri's. Of the eighth-verse names (Kriya, Tāvuri and so on) only the existence was noted. I did not open the Neely English.
- **Jātakapārijāta**: the Sanskrit lines are badly garbled in the OCR; the Sanskrit labels in the JP sections are the terms used in Sastri's English or transliterations in his notes. Volume 2 was not opened, so any ascendant-by-ascendant chapter outside Adhyāya IX of volume 1 was not read. The Moon-in-sign verses (Adhyāya IX, slokas 91–93) describe the person born with the Moon in a sign, and are listed as such.
- **Phaladīpikā**: the Sanskrit was not opened. Chapters other than 1 and 9 (for instance the chapter on diseases by planets and signs) were not read for sign qualities. Kapoor's English of I.8 lists "Taurus, Cancer, Aries and Capricorn" in its first sentence and omits Sagittarius there; Sastri's lists all five.
- **BPHS Sanskrit edition**: the edition behind [E] is not named on the site, and its verse numbering is the site's own; the English printed beside it is automatically made and was not used. Where Santhanam's English and the Sanskrit differ (Mīna's day or night, Dhanus's rising), I give both and decide neither.
- **Terms from memory**: only the Sanskrit–English glosses of dictionary words such as karbura (variegated, spotted, tawny) and the remark on the common four-element scheme are from memory; every other Sanskrit word in this file is printed in a passage I opened, in the Sanskrit lines of [E] and [BJ] or in the translators' transliterations.

### The five mahābhūtas (great elements) and the three guṇas (strands of nature) as the Sanskrit texts give them to the eye and the hand

This part collects what the texts say; it states no assignment of its own, draws no conclusion and sets no rule. Where the texts differ, the readings are laid side by side and each is named.

Sanskrit is in IAST, each term followed by its English. The five mahābhūtas are pṛthivī (earth), āpas (water), tejas (fire; also agni), vāyu (air) and ākāśa (space, ether). The three guṇas are sattva (lucidity, goodness), rajas (activity, passion) and tamas (inertia, darkness).

#### How the source marks work

- `(read: TEXT chapter.verse [KEY])` means I opened that passage. `[KEY]` stands for the URL listed under "Sources opened" below. The verse number is the one in the edition I opened; editions number differently (the Gheraṇḍa Saṃhitā passage is 3.70–80 in the editions opened, not 3.69–77).
- `(memory)` means the statement comes from memory or a secondary summary, not from a passage I opened. Almost nothing below is marked this way; where a passage could not be opened, the gap is listed at the end.
- Most texts were opened as OCR text of scanned books, or as web transcriptions. Where a line was damaged or ambiguous, I say so. English renderings in quotation marks are the translators' words; where I give "gloss", it is my literal rendering of Sanskrit I read.
