#### The three colours that stand behind the later colour schemes

**Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.4 (red, white, black).** Uddālaka teaches that fire, water and earth ("food", *anna*) each have a colour, and that everything seen is a combination of these three:

- Red is the colour of fire; white the colour of water; black the colour of earth. The text says it of burning fire, of the sun, of the moon and of lightning in turn, and ends: "What is true are the three colours" (read: Chāndogya 6.4.1–4 [CU]).
- "Whatever they thought looked red, they knew was the colour of fire. Whatever they thought looked white, they knew was the colour of water. Whatever they thought looked black, they knew was the colour of earth." (read: Chāndogya 6.4.6 [CU])
- Müller's notes on the passage: fire "is whatever burns, cooks, shines, and is red"; water "all that is fluid, and bright in colour"; earth/food "all that is heavy, firm, dark in colour" (read: Chāndogya 6.3–6.4, Müller's notes [CU]). These are the translator's glosses, not the verse.
- The eaten substance: the subtlest part of earth becomes mind, of water becomes breath, of fire becomes speech (read: Chāndogya 6.5.1–4 and 6.6.2–5 [CU]).
- In the Upaniṣad the three are *tejas* (fire, heat-light), *āpas* (water) and *anna* (food, earth). No colour is given to air or space in this passage.

**Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.5 (lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇā).** "There is one unborn being (female), red, white, and black, uniform, but producing manifold offspring" (read: Śvetāśvatara 4.5 [SU]).

- Müller's note says the reading is *rohita-kṛṣṇa-śukla* (red-black-white), with the variant *lohita-kṛṣṇa-varṇa*, and that the three colours are "explained as signifying either the three guṇas, rajas, sattva, and tamas, or better (Chāndogya VI, 3, 1), the three elements, tejas (fire), ap (water), and anna (earth)" (read: Śvetāśvatara 4.5, note [SU]).
- The Sāṃkhya reading (the three colours are the guṇas) and the Vedānta reading (the three are the colours of fire, water, earth, after Chāndogya 6.4) are both named in the editor's introduction to the Sāṃkhya Kārikā, which says Śaṅkara takes the second (read: Suryanarayana Sastri, Introduction, notes 10–11 [SK]).
- In this verse the colours are listed in the order red, white, black; the guṇas in Müller's gloss come in the order rajas, sattva, tamas (read [SU]).

---

#### The five mahābhūtas

Each element below lists: colour (varṇa); shape (maṇḍala, ākṛti); place or direction; size or measure; touch, texture and felt quality; sense (tanmātra) and taste; lustre, metal, gem and similes; temperament the text names.

##### Pṛthivī (earth)

**Colour (varṇa).**
- Black, in Chāndogya 6.4 (read: 6.4.1–6 [CU]).
- Yellow like orpiment (*haritāla*, arsenic sulphide): Gheraṇḍa Saṃhitā in both English renderings, and the Sanskrit reads *haritāla-deśa-racitaṃ bhaumaṃ lakārānvitaṃ* (gloss: "the earthy principle formed of the orpiment-region, joined with the letter la") (read: Gheraṇḍa Saṃhitā 3.70 [GhV], [GhM], [GhS]).
- Yellow (*pītā bhūmiḥ*): Śāradā Tilaka 1.21–22a (read [ST]).
- Yellow: Yogatattva Upaniṣad (read [YT]); Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.5, "shining yellow colour" "beautiful like lightning" (read [SC]); Svarodaya v.151, 161, 166 (read [SV]).
- Many colours, "blue, yellow, etc." belong to earth alone, in the commentary on Vaiśeṣika Sūtra 2.1.1 (read [VS]).
- Soil: white, red, yellow or black earth is praised for the four varṇas (*sita-rakta-pīta-kṛṣṇā viprādīnāṃ praśasyate bhūmiḥ*), and its smell is like ghee, blood, food or liquor (read: Bṛhat Saṃhitā 52.94 [BS]). This is the earth of a building site; it is not given as the colour of the element.

**Shape (maṇḍala).**
- Square ("four-sided") in Gheraṇḍa 3.70 [GhV], [GhM]; "four-sided" in Yogatattva [YT]; quadrangular in Svarodaya v.149, 166 [SV].
- Square with eight vajras (thunderbolts; *caturasraṃ savajrakam*), Śāradā Tilaka 1.24; Rāghavabhaṭṭa discusses whether the vajras are drawn on the lines or at the corners (read [ST]).
- Square with eight shining spears around it, in the pericarp of the Mūlādhāra lotus, Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.5 (read [SC]).
- In Rama Prasad's commentary the vibration of earth is drawn as a quadrangle (read [SV], commentary).

**Place, direction, motion.**
- Heart (Gheraṇḍa 3.70 [GhV], [GhM]). Feet to knees (Yogatattva [YT]). Feet (Svarodaya v.153 [SV]). The root centre, Mūlādhāra (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa [SC]).
- Direction of flow "midway" (Svarodaya v.150); "in the west" (v.171); "slow motion, moves in the middle" (v.161) (read [SV]).
- Heavy, "naturally gravitates downward" with water (read: Suśruta Sūtra 41 [SS]).

**Size or measure.** The breath-flow of the earth tattva is "twelve fingers" (Svarodaya v.155, 166 [SV]). Held for five ghaṭikās (about two hours) in Gheraṇḍa and two hours in Yogatattva (read [GhV], [GhM], [YT]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality (guṇa).**
- Caraka, drugs of earth: "heavy, coarse, hard, dull, stable, non-slimy, solid, gross"; they act by "development, compactness, heaviness and firmness" (read: Caraka Sūtra 26.11 [CS]).
- In the body: "particularly thick, stable, massive, heavy, coarse and hard"; nails, bones, teeth, flesh, skin, faeces, hair, tendons, with smell and the sense of smell (read: Caraka Śārīra 7.16 [CS]).
- "Coarseness" is the specific mark of pṛthivī (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.29–30 [CS]).
- Suśruta: "thick, pithy, compact, dull, immobile, rough, heavy, strong smelling", largely sweet with a shade of astringent; it increases "firmness, strength, hardness and rotundity" (read: Suśruta Sūtra 41 [SS]).
- Touch (*sparśa*) is also an attribute of earth, with colour, taste and smell (read: Vaiśeṣika 2.1.1 [VS]).
- Heat: "slight heat in temperature" (Svarodaya v.161 [SV]).

**Sense and taste.** Smell (*gandha*) is the guṇa of pṛthivī, the last of the five: "sound, touch, colour, taste, smell" are the guṇas of the five bhūtas in the order ākāśa to pṛthivī (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.27 [CS]; Śāradā Tilaka 1.20–22 [ST]; Svarodaya commentary [SV]). The smell is of two kinds, fragrant and non-fragrant (read: Vaiśeṣika 2.1.1 commentary [VS]). Taste: sweet (read: Svarodaya v.154, 166 [SV]; Suśruta 41 [SS]).

**Lustre, metal, gem, simile.**
- Orpiment (a yellow mineral) in the Gheraṇḍa; "of a golden colour" is said of Brahmā who is contemplated there (read: Yogatattva [YT]).
- Meditation on the earth tattva "conferring a colour as pure as that of gold" (read: Svarodaya v.203 [SV]).

**Temperament named.**
- Steadiness: the earth dhāraṇā "causes steadiness" (read: Gheraṇḍa 3.70 [GhV]; [GhM] "steadiness"); the Sanskrit has *stambha-karīṃ* (gloss: "producing rigidity, steadiness") (read [GhS]).
- "Acts which are expected to live long", success in what is to last (Svarodaya v.157, 161); "the middle one to endurance" (v.156); "giver of enjoyment" (v.166) (read [SV]).
- "Auspicious" with water; "income is late" (read: Svarodaya v.170, 160 [SV]).

##### Āpas (water)

**Colour.**
- White in Chāndogya 6.4 (read [CU]).
- Gheraṇḍa: "white like the Kunda-flower or a conch or the moon" [GhV]; "as white as a jasmine flower" [GhM]; Sanskrit *śaṅkhendu-pratimaṃ ca kunda-dhavalaṃ*, gloss "like a conch and the moon, white as the kunda flower" [GhS] (all read, 3.72).
- White: Yogatattva, "the colour of pure crystal" for the deity Nārāyaṇa, the element itself white (read [YT]); Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.15, "white, shining" and the bīja "stainless and white as the autumnal moon" (read [SC]); Svarodaya v.151, 162, 167 (read [SV]).
- Śāradā Tilaka 1.21: *viśadaṃ payaḥ* ("clear, bright water"); Rāghavabhaṭṭa explains the colour words in this verse (read [ST]).
- Vaiśeṣika commentary: colour white, taste sweet, touch cool; the commentary meets the objection that the Yamunā is blue and gives reasons (read: 2.1.2 [VS]).

**Shape.**
- Half-moon (*ardhendu*): Gheraṇḍa in Mallinson's English, "resembles a half-moon" [GhM]; Yogatattva "semi-lunar" [YT]; Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.15 "of the shape of a half-moon" [SC]; Svarodaya v.149, 167 "semi-lunar" [SV]; Śāradā Tilaka 1.23–24 *ardhendu-saṃyutam ambhojam* (gloss: "the lotus joined with the half-moon") [ST]. The commentary gives the figure as a half-moon with two lotuses, rejects the view that it is an eight-petal lotus with crescents at the petal tips, and cites other texts for the half-moon with a lotus (read [ST]).
- Circular "like the moon": Vasu's English of Gheraṇḍa 3.72 [GhV]. The Sanskrit as I read it has only *śaṅkhendu-pratimaṃ*, "like a conch and the moon", with no separate word for the shape [GhS].
- Eight-cornered (*aṣṭāsra*): reported by Woodroffe for the *Viśvasāra Tantra* (read: note to Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.14 [SC]; I did not open the Tantra).

**Place, direction, motion.**
- Throat (Gheraṇḍa [GhM]); heart (Gheraṇḍa [GhV]); knees to anus (Yogatattva [YT]); knees (Svarodaya v.153 [SV]); the Svādhiṣṭhāna centre at the root of the genitals (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa [SC]).
- "Flows downwards" (v.150, 162); "in the east" (v.171) (read: Svarodaya [SV]). "Downward to calmness" (v.156) [SV].
- Heavy, gravitates downward (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]).

**Size or measure.** The breath-flow of apas is "sixteen fingers" (Svarodaya v.155, 162, 167 [SV]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality.**
- Caraka, drugs of water: "liquid, unctuous, cold, dull, soft, slimy"; they act by "moistening, unction, binding, oozing, softening and exhilaration" (read: Caraka Sūtra 26.11 [CS]).
- Specific mark: "liquidity" (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.29–30 [CS]).
- Suśruta: "cold, moist, glossy, devoid of keenness, takes time to be digested, is mobile, compact, soft, slimy, sappy", with acid, saline or sweet taste (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]).
- Vaiśeṣika: fluid (*drava*) and viscid (*snigdha*) (read: 2.1.2 [VS]).
- Heat: "cool in temperature", "the coolest" (read: Svarodaya v.162 [SV]).

**Sense and taste.** Taste (*rasa*) is the guṇa of water (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.27 [CS]; Śāradā Tilaka 1.20 [ST]). Taste: astringent in Svarodaya v.154, 167 [SV]; "sweet" for the soma (water) element in Caraka Sūtra 26.40 [CS] (see the taste table below).

**Lustre, metal, gem, simile.** Conch, moon, kunda flower, jasmine (Gheraṇḍa [GhV], [GhM]); "pure crystal" and "orange clothes" for the deity (Yogatattva [YT]); the water region is "luminously white" (*viśada-prakāśa*) (read: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.15, commentary [SC]).

**Temperament named.**
- Gheraṇḍa: the water dhāraṇā "destroyer of all sorrows" [GhV]; "removes unbearable suffering and sins" [GhM]. Svarodaya v.162: "gives success in auspicious works"; v.157: "passing acts"; v.167: "the causer of benefit" (read [SV]).
- Meditation gives "endurance of hunger and thirst" and "a sensation similar to that of a plunge in water" (read: Svarodaya v.204 [SV]).

##### Tejas (fire; also agni)

**Colour.**
- Red in Chāndogya 6.4 (read [CU]).
- Gheraṇḍa 3.75: "red like the Indra-gopa insect" [GhV]; "blazing red" [GhM]; Sanskrit *-gopa-sadṛśaṃ … pradīptam aruṇam*, gloss "like the (Indra)gopa insect … blazing, ruddy", with the lord Rudra [GhS]. (Footnote in [GhM]: the Indragopa is a cochineal-type insect "bright red".)
- Red: Śāradā Tilaka 1.21 (*rakto 'gniḥ*) [ST]; Yogatattva (the deity Rudra "of the colour of the midday sun") [YT]; Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.19, "triangular in form and shining like the rising sun" and "the red Region of Fire" in the commentary [SC]; Svarodaya v.151, 163 [SV].
- Vaiśeṣika commentary: "Colour which is luminous, and Touch which is hot" (read: 2.1.3 [VS]).

**Shape.**
- Triangle: Gheraṇḍa [GhV], [GhM], [GhS]; Yogatattva "triangular" [YT]; Svarodaya v.149 [SV].
- Triangle "with svastikas" (*trikoṇaṃ svastikopetam*), Śāradā Tilaka 1.23; Rāghavabhaṭṭa says the apex is upward, citing "fire above, śakti below" (*ūrdhvaṃ vahnir adhaḥ śaktiḥ*) (read [ST]).
- Triangle with three svastikas outside it, in the Maṇipūra lotus (read: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.19 [SC]).

**Place, direction, motion.**
- Navel (Gheraṇḍa [GhV], [GhS]); palate (Gheraṇḍa [GhM]); anus to heart (Yogatattva [YT]); between the shoulders (Svarodaya v.153 [SV]); Maṇipūra at the navel (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa [SC]).
- "Flows upwards" (v.150); "moves in whirls (*āvartagaḥ*), moves upwards" (v.163); "in the south" (v.171) (read: Svarodaya [SV]). "The upward motion tends to death" (v.156) [SV].
- Light, ascends (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]).

**Size or measure.** Breath-flow "four fingers" (Svarodaya v.155, 163 [SV]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality.**
- Caraka: "hot, sharp, minute, light, rough, non-slimy"; acts: "heat, digestion, lustre, light and complexion" (read: Caraka Sūtra 26.11 [CS]). Specific mark: "hotness" (read: Śārīra 1.29–30 [CS]).
- Suśruta: "heat-making, pungent and keen, subtle in its essence, permeates the minutest capillaries, … dry, rough, light, and non-slimy"; it "improves the complexion and imparts a healthful glow" (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]).
- Heat: "very high in temperature" (read: Svarodaya v.163 [SV]); "the hottest" (read: Rama Prasad's commentary [SV]).

**Sense and taste.** Colour or form (*rūpa*) is the guṇa of fire (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.27 [CS]; Śāradā Tilaka 1.20 [ST]). Taste: pungent (Svarodaya v.154 [SV]); sharp/pungent with a shade of saline (Suśruta 41 [SS]).

**Lustre, metal, gem, simile.** "Refulgent like the sun" (Gheraṇḍa [GhV]); Indragopa insect; the Maṇipūra lotus is called after "maṇi" (gem) because the presence of fiery tejas makes it "lustrous as a gem", a derivation Woodroffe cites from the Gautamīya Tantra (read: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa, commentary [SC]).

**Temperament named.**
- Gheraṇḍa: "the giver of success" and destroyer of "the fear of dreadful death" [GhV]; "removes the deep fear of death" [GhM].
- Svarodaya v.163: "gives birth to harsh actions [actions which, so to say, set one on fire]" (the bracket is Rama Prasad's); v.157 "harsh acts"; v.170 "moderate in its effects"; v.205: meditation gives "the power of consuming a great amount of food and drink, and the endurance of burning heat" (read [SV]).

##### Vāyu (air; also marut, pavana)

**Colour.**
- Black, "as unguent for the eyes" (collyrium): Gheraṇḍa 3.77, Vasu [GhV]; "like a mass of lampblack" [GhM]; Sanskrit *bhinnāñjana-puñja-sannibham … dhūmrāvabhāsam*, gloss "like a heap of crushed collyrium, smoky in appearance" [GhS].
- Black (*kṛṣṇo marut*): Śāradā Tilaka 1.21 [ST]; black: Yogatattva (hexangular, black) [YT].
- Smoke-coloured: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.22, "six-cornered … like unto the smoke in colour"; the commentary says the smoke colour is "by reason of its being surrounded by masses of vapour" (read [SC]); Woodroffe's note reports the Śāradā as giving black (*kṛṣṇa*) (read [SC]).
- Sky-blue: Svarodaya v.151, 164, 168, 206 (read [SV]). Green: in Rama Prasad's own table of the colours of the prāṇa (his commentary) and in the introduction to a modern edition (read [SV], [SVI]; the layout of that table in the scan is damaged).

**Shape.**
- Circle (the text says "a circle"): Gheraṇḍa in Mallinson's English [GhM]. Vasu's English gives no shape [GhV]; the Sanskrit as I read it has none [GhS]. Mallinson's footnote 4 says that in other texts the air element is visualised as a ring of six dots (read [GhM]).
- Hexagonal: Yogatattva [YT]; Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.22, "six-cornered, formed by two triangles, of which one is inverted" (read [SC]).
- A circle "marked with six dots" (*vṛttaṃ … ṣaḍ-bindu-lāñchitaṃ mātariśvanaḥ*): Śāradā Tilaka 1.23, where the circle is first given to the sky (*divaḥ*) and then "that" circle, with six dots, to air (read [ST]).
- Spherical: Svarodaya v.149, 168, 206 [SV].

**Place, direction, motion.**
- Between the eyebrows (Gheraṇḍa [GhM]); heart to the middle of the eyebrows (Yogatattva [YT]); root of the navel (Svarodaya v.153 [SV]); Anāhata at the heart (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa [SC]). Vasu's English names no place [GhV].
- "Flows at acute angles" (v.150, 164); "in the north" (v.171); "the one at acute angles to restlessness" (v.156) (read: Svarodaya [SV]).
- The bīja is seated on a black antelope "noted for its fleetness … with its property of motion" (read: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.22, commentary [SC]).
- Light, ascends (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]).

**Size or measure.** Breath-flow "eight fingers" (Svarodaya v.155, 164, 168 [SV]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality.**
- Caraka: "light, cold, rough, coarse, non-slimy, minute"; acts: "roughness, depression, movement, non-sliminess and lightness" (read: Sūtra 26.11 [CS]). Specific mark: "mobility" (read: Śārīra 1.29–30 [CS]).
- Suśruta: "subtle in its essence … dry, rough, light, cold and non-slimy, increases tactual sensation"; it removes sliminess and produces "lightness, dryness and emaciation" (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]).
- Vaiśeṣika: touch alone; a touch "not co-existent with colour"; the scan of the commentary is partly illegible, and I read in it "neither hot nor cold" (read: 2.1.4 [VS]).
- Heat: "hot or cool" (read: Svarodaya v.164 [SV]); "becomes cool" (read: Rama Prasad's commentary [SV]).

**Sense and taste.** Touch (*sparśa*) is the guṇa of air (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.27 [CS]; Śāradā Tilaka 1.20 [ST]). Taste: acid (sour) in Svarodaya v.154, 168 [SV]; astringent with a shade of bitter in Suśruta 41 [SS].

**Lustre, metal, gem, simile.** Collyrium, lampblack, smoke, a black antelope (above).

**Temperament named.**
- Gheraṇḍa: "full of Satva quality" (Vasu's English of *sattvamayam*, gloss "made of sattva") (read: 3.77 [GhV], [GhS]); the dhāraṇā gives the power to move through space [GhM].
- Svarodaya v.157: "killing, etc."; v.164 "transitory" works; v.170 "inauspicious … cause loss and death"; v.159 "reduction" (read [SV]).
- Suśruta: it "increases the speculative or contemplative faculty of the mind" (read: Sūtra 41 [SS]).

##### Ākāśa (space, ether; also vyoman, nabhas, viyat)

**Colour.**
- Pure-water colour: Gheraṇḍa 3.80, "the colour of pure sea-water" [GhV]; "the clearest water" [GhM]; *śuddha-vāri-sadṛśaṃ* [GhS].
- *Svaccha* (clear, transparent), Śāradā Tilaka 1.21; Rāghavabhaṭṭa glosses "*svacchaṃ śvetam*" ("*svaccha* is white") and says the colours of the bodiless (*arūpi*) elements are named for worship, following each tradition's own text (read [ST]); Woodroffe reports it as "transparent (*svaccha*)" (read [SC]).
- White: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.28–29, "white like the full Moon", with the bīja on a white elephant (read [SC]); the diagram is "a transparent white circular diagram in which, according to some accounts, there are dots" (read: Woodroffe, Introduction [SC]).
- Smoky: Yogatattva (read [YT]).
- Svarodaya: "foreshadows every colour" (v.151, 165, 169); "spotted" (v.147, 149); "formless, foreshadowing many colours" (v.207) (read [SV]). In Rama Prasad's commentary the colour is "dark" (read [SV]).
- Vaiśeṣika: "colour, taste, smell and touch" are not in ether; the commentary meets the objection that ether looks white as curd or blue, by saying the impression comes from the sun's rays or the emerald peak on Sumeru (read: 2.1.5 [VS]).

**Shape.**
- None given: Gheraṇḍa [GhV], [GhM], [GhS]. Mallinson's footnote 4: "No shape is given here … In other texts it is circular" (read [GhM]).
- Circular: Yogatattva [YT]; Śāradā Tilaka 1.23 *vṛttaṃ divaḥ* (gloss: "the circle is the sky's") [ST]; Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.28–29 [SC].
- "Spotted"; "of the shape of an ear"; "formless" (Svarodaya v.149, 169, 207 [SV]); an oval or dotted figure and "multifarious" in the English introduction's table (read [SVI]).

**Place, direction, motion.**
- At the aperture of Brahman, the crown (Gheraṇḍa [GhM]); middle of the eyebrows to the top of the head (Yogatattva [YT]); the head (Svarodaya v.153 [SV]); the Viśuddha lotus at the throat (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa [SC]).
- "Flows between every two" (v.150); "in the middle" (v.171); "moving everywhere" (v.169) (read: Svarodaya [SV]).

**Size or measure.** No flow measure in the verses I read; Svarodaya v.155 lists four elements only (read [SV]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality.**
- Caraka: "soft, light, minute, smooth and sound"; acts: "softening, hollowing and lightness" (read: Sūtra 26.11 [CS]). Specific mark: "non-interruption" (*apratighāta*, "non-obstruction", Sharma's "non-interruption") (read: Śārīra 1.29–30 [CS]).
- Suśruta: "smooth, unctuous … subtle … soft or pliant … expansive … porous, soundy … without any definite taste" (read: Sūtra 41 [SS]).
- Heat: "neither cools nor heats" in Rama Prasad's commentary (read [SV]).

**Sense and taste.** Sound (*śabda*) is the guṇa of ākāśa, the first and single (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.27–28 [CS]). "Each later bhūta adds one more property to the one before" (read: Caraka Śārīra 1.28 [CS]). Taste: bitter (Svarodaya v.154 [SV]); none definite (Suśruta 41 [SS]). Caraka Sūtra 26.40 assigns the bitter taste to vāyu and ākāśa together [CS].

**Lustre, metal, gem, simile.** "Pure sea-water" (Vasu); "clearest water"; for the deity Sadāśiva, "shining like pure crystal" and "wearing the rising crescent of moon" (Yogatattva [YT]); "white like the full Moon" (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa [SC]).

**Temperament named.**
- Gheraṇḍa: it "opens the gates of emancipation" [GhV]; "break down the doorway to liberation" [GhM].
- Svarodaya v.158: "Nothing ought to be done during the Ākāśa except the practice of Yoga"; v.165 "gives Yoga to the Yogis"; v.169 "giver of Moksha … useless in all worldly works" (read [SV]).

##### Where the texts differ: colour and shape of the five bhūtas

| Text | Pṛthivī | Āpas | Tejas | Vāyu | Ākāśa |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Chāndogya 6.4 [CU] | black | white | red | not given | not given |
| Gheraṇḍa, Vasu [GhV] | yellow, four-sided | white; "circular like the moon" | red, triangular | black as collyrium; no shape | pure sea-water colour; no shape |
| Gheraṇḍa, Mallinson [GhM] | yellow, square | white, half-moon | red, triangle | lampblack, circle | clearest water; no shape |
| Yogatattva [YT] | yellow, four-sided | white, semi-lunar | red, triangular | black, hexangular | smoky, circular |
| Śāradā Tilaka 1.21–24 [ST] | yellow, square with vajras | *viśada* (clear, bright), half-moon with lotus | red, triangle with svastikas | black, circle marked with six dots | *svaccha* ("white" in the commentary), circle |
| Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa 5, 15, 19, 22, 28–29 [SC] | yellow, square with eight spears | white, half-moon | red, triangle | smoke-coloured, six-cornered | white, circle |
| Svarodaya (Rama Prasad) v.149–151 [SV] | yellow, quadrangular | white, semi-lunar | red, triangular | sky-blue, spherical | all colours foreshadowed; spotted, ear-shaped or formless |

- On the Śāradā Tilaka's colour for water, Woodroffe's note gives "white (*śveta*)" and for ākāśa "transparent (*svaccha*)" (read [SC]); Rāghavabhaṭṭa's gloss "*svaccha* is white" is for ākāśa (read [ST]). The verse itself says *viśadaṃ payaḥ* for water.
- The English introduction's table (page 20) lists, under shape: square, half-moon, triangular, "hexagonal or round", "multifarious"; under colour: yellow, white, red, green; and the layout in the scan is damaged, so the colour of ākāśa and the order of its taste column cannot be read with certainty (read [SVI]).
- For the Gheraṇḍa, two renderings and one Sanskrit transcription give different places: earth at the heart in all; water at the heart in Vasu [GhV] and at the throat in Mallinson [GhM]; fire at the navel in Vasu [GhV] and the Sanskrit [GhS], at the palate in Mallinson [GhM]; air between the eyebrows in Mallinson [GhM], unstated in Vasu; ākāśa at the aperture of Brahman in Mallinson, unstated in Vasu.

##### The tastes by bhūta in three texts

| Text | Pṛthivī | Āpas | Tejas | Vāyu | Ākāśa |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Svarodaya v.154 [SV] | sweet | astringent | pungent | acid | bitter |
| Suśruta Sūtra 41 [SS] | sweet, with a shade of astringent | acid, saline or sweet, with a shade of astringent | pungent, with a shade of saline | astringent, with a shade of bitter | no definite taste |
| Caraka Sūtra 26.40 [CS] (each taste by the bhūtas that predominate in it) | amla (sour) with agni; kaṣāya (astringent) with vāyu | madhura (sweet) by soma (āpas); lavaṇa (saline) with agni | amla with pṛthivī; lavaṇa with āpas; kaṭu (pungent) with vāyu | kaṭu with agni; tikta (bitter) with ākāśa; kaṣāya with pṛthivī | tikta with vāyu |

The Caraka row is read from the English: madhura is produced by the predominance of soma (āpas); amla by pṛthivī and agni; lavaṇa by āpas and agni; kaṭu by vāyu and agni; tikta by vāyu and ākāśa; kaṣāya by vāyu and pṛthivī. Each cell lists the tastes in whose making that bhūta takes part. Caraka adds that the six forms of manifestation come about "according to shortage and excess of mahābhūtas like various complexions and shapes in plants and animals" (read: Caraka Sūtra 26.40 [CS]).

---

#### The three guṇas

The texts give no size or measure to the guṇas. Their marks are felt, seen as light, or shown in the mind and conduct they bring. Where a colour is given, it is named below with its source.

##### Sattva (lucidity, goodness)

**Colour.**
- White (*śukla*): the sound *u* of the praṇava is "*sāttvika*, white, and is called Viṣṇu" (*ukāraḥ sāttvikaḥ śuklo viṣṇur ity abhidhīyate*) (read: Yogacūḍāmaṇi Upaniṣad, verse 75 as printed [YC]). The same passage says "*sāttviko viṣṇuḥ*" for the lord of preservation.
- In the three-coloured verse (Śvetāśvatara 4.5), one reading takes the white as sattva (see above) (read [SU], [SK]).
- The Gītā and the Bhāgavata chapters opened name no colour for the guṇas (read: Gītā 14, 17, 18 [BG]; Bhāgavata 11.25 [BP]).

**Shape, direction.**
- "Those established in sattva go upward" (*ūrdhvaṃ gacchanti sattvasthāḥ*) (read: Gītā 14.18 [BG]); the learned "elevated to higher and higher positions" (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.21 [BP]); "those who leave this world in sattva go to the heavenly planets" (read: 11.25.22 [BP]).
- The editor's note to Sāṃkhya Kārikā 13 says sattva is "responsible for the lightness in things, the upward movement of the burning fire or the blowing across of the wind" (read [SK], note; this is the commentary's reading, not the verse).

**Touch, texture, felt quality (guṇa).**
- *laghu* (light) and *prakāśaka* (illuminating): "sattvaṃ laghu prakāśakam iṣṭam" ("sattva is held to be light and illuminating") (read: Sāṃkhya Kārikā 13 [SK]); the tripartite function "like a lamp", wick, oil and flame together giving light (read: SK 13 and notes [SK]).
- *nirmala* (stainless, pure), *prakāśaka* (illuminating), *anāmaya* (free from illness) (read: Gītā 14.6 [BG]); "light … arises in all the gates of the body" (*sarvadvāreṣu dehe 'smin prakāśa upajāyate*) (read: 14.11 [BG]).
- *bhāsvara* (luminous), *viśada* (clear, pure), *śiva* (auspicious) (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.13 [BP]); "when consciousness becomes clear" (*cittaṃ prasīdeta*) (read: 11.25.16 [BP]).
- Food of sattva: *rasyāḥ snigdhāḥ sthirā hṛdyāḥ* (gloss: "juicy, oily, firm, agreeable to the heart"), which increase life, vigour, strength, health, joy and liking (*āyuḥ-sattva-balārogya-sukha-prīti-vivardhanāḥ*) (read: Gītā 17.8 [BG]). Bhāgavata: "wholesome, pure and obtained without difficulty" (read: 11.25.28 [BP]).
- Sāṃkhya: "of the nature of pleasure" (*prīti*); serves to illumine; "buoyant and illuminating" (read: Sāṃkhya Kārikā 12–13 [SK]).

**Temperament and feeling named.**
- Pleasure (*sukha*) and knowledge: "it binds by attachment to happiness and to knowledge" (read: Gītā 14.6 [BG]); light (*prakāśa*) and knowledge arise at all the gates of the body when sattva grows (14.11 [BG]); "from sattva arises knowledge" (14.17 [BG]).
- Qualities listed in Bhāgavata 11.25.2–5: mind and sense control, tolerance, discrimination, truthfulness, mercy, satisfaction, generosity, simplicity, humbleness, "satisfaction within oneself" (read [BP], Bhaktivedanta's English).
- Sattva-minded types in Caraka: "clean, true to his word, having controlled his self … devoid of passion, anger, greed, conceit"; seven sub-types after Brahmā, a sage, Indra, Yama, Varuṇa, Kubera and a Gandharva, each with its own temper; the Varuṇa type "liking for entertainment in water", the Gandharva "dance, music … perfumes, garlands" (read: Caraka Śārīra 4.36–37 [CS]).
- Gītā 14.9 (sattva "attaches to happiness"); 17.14–16 (the tapas of body, speech and mind: worship of gods and teachers, purity, straightness; truthful, pleasant speech; "serenity of mind, gentleness, silence, self-control, purity of feeling"); 17.17 (tapas "with supreme faith … by those unattached to the fruit" is sāttvika); 18.37 (sattva happiness "like poison at first, like nectar in the end"); 18.20, 18.23, 18.26, 18.30, 18.33 (undivided view, unattached action, unattached doer, knowing what to do and not do, steadfast hold) (read [BG]).
- Residence in the forest is sattvic (*vanaṃ tu sāttviko vāsaḥ*) (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.25 [BP]); waking (*jāgaraṇa*) comes from sattva (11.25.20 [BP]).
- The deities who grow in strength with sattva are the gods (read: 11.25.19 [BP]).

##### Rajas (activity, passion)

**Colour.**
- Red (*rakta*): the sound *a* of the praṇava is "*rājasa*, red, called Brahmā" (*akāro rājaso rakto brahma cetana ucyate*) (read: Yogacūḍāmaṇi Upaniṣad, verse 75 as printed [YC]).
- In Śvetāśvatara 4.5 the red (*lohita*, *rohita*), by the Sāṃkhya reading, is rajas (read [SU], [SK]); the red of the Chāndogya is the colour of fire (read: 6.4 [CU]).

**Shape, direction.** "The rājasa stay in the middle" (*madhye tiṣṭhanti rājasāḥ*) (read: Gītā 14.18 [BG]); the passionate remain in the world of human beings (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.21–22 [BP]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality.**
- *upaṣṭambhaka* (stimulating, propping up) and *cala* (mobile): "*upaṣṭambhakaṃ calaṃ ca rajaḥ*" (read: Sāṃkhya Kārikā 13 [SK]); serves "to actuate" (*pravṛtti*), of the nature of pain (*apriti*) (read: SK 12 [SK]). The editor's note: neither tamas nor sattva "would have the energy to fulfil its proper functions but for the stimulating activity of Rajas" (read [SK], note).
- *rāgātmaka* (of the nature of passion), arising from thirst (*tṛṣṇā*) and attachment (*saṅga*) (read: Gītā 14.7 [BG]); "greed, activity, undertaking of works, restlessness, craving" (*lobhaḥ pravṛttir ārambhaḥ karmaṇām aśamaḥ spṛhā*) (read: 14.12 [BG]).
- *saṅgaṃ bhidā calam* (gloss: "attachment, separation, moving") (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.14 [BP]); "distortion of the intelligence because of too much activity … the unsteady perplexity of the mind" (read: 11.25.17 [BP]).
- Food of rajas: *kaṭv-amla-lavaṇāty-uṣṇa-tīkṣṇa-rūkṣa-vidāhinaḥ* (gloss: "bitter/pungent, sour, salty, very hot, sharp, dry, burning"), giving pain, grief and illness (read: Gītā 17.9 [BG]). Bhāgavata: "gives immediate pleasure to the senses" (read: 11.25.28 [BP]).
- The rājasa tapas is "unsteady, impermanent" (*calam adhruvam*) (read: Gītā 17.18 [BG]).

**Temperament and feeling named.**
- Pain (*duḥkha*) as the fruit (read: Gītā 14.16 [BG]); "greed" from rajas (14.17 [BG]); passion, "dream sleep" (*svapna*) from rajas (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.20 [BP]).
- Qualities in Bhāgavata 11.25.2–5: material desire, great endeavor, audacity, false pride, "a fondness for hearing oneself praised", a tendency to ridicule others (read [BP]).
- Caraka lists six rajas types: asura, rākṣasa, piśāca, sarpa (serpent), preta, śakuna (bird), each a mixture of agitation, cruelty, fear, greed, etc.; the sarpa "brave in angry condition and timid otherwise, sharp, exertive" (read: Śārīra 4.38 [CS]).
- Gītā 17.12, 17.21, 18.21, 18.24, 18.27, 18.31, 18.34, 18.38 (the rājasa offering for a fruit; knowledge as separateness; action with much effort; the doer "attached, desiring the fruit of action, greedy, violent, impure, swayed by joy and grief"; happiness "like nectar at first, like poison in the end") (read [BG]).
- Residence in a town (*grāmo rājasa ucyate*) (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.25 [BP]); the *asura* (demoniac) strengthen with rajas (11.25.19 [BP]).

##### Tamas (inertia, darkness)

**Colour.**
- Black (*kṛṣṇa*): the sound *m* of the praṇava is "*tāmasa*, black, called Rudra" (*makāras tāmasaḥ kṛṣṇo rudraś ceti tathocyate*) (read: Yogacūḍāmaṇi Upaniṣad, verse 75–76 as printed [YC]).
- In Śvetāśvatara 4.5 the black (*kṛṣṇa*), by the Sāṃkhya reading, is tamas (read [SU], [SK]); the black of the Chāndogya is the colour of earth (read: 6.4 [CU]).

**Shape, direction.** "The tāmasa, in the lowest strand's way of being, go downward" (*jaghanyaguṇavṛttisthā adho gacchanti tāmasāḥ*) (read: Gītā 14.18 [BG]); "forces one to fall headfirst into lower and lower births" (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.21 [BP]); "must go to hell" (11.25.22 [BP]).

**Touch, texture, felt quality.**
- *guru* (heavy) and *varaṇaka* (enveloping, covering): "*guru varaṇakam eva tamaḥ*" (read: Sāṃkhya Kārikā 13 [SK]); serves "to restrain" (*niyama*), of the nature of indifference or dejection (*viṣāda*) (read: SK 12 [SK]). The editor's note: tamas "weighs down things and renders them inactive" (read [SK], note).
- *ajñānaja* (born of ignorance), *mohana* (bewildering) for all embodied beings, binding by carelessness (*pramāda*), laziness (*ālasya*) and sleep (*nidrā*) (read: Gītā 14.8 [BG]); "absence of light" (*aprakāśa*), inactivity, carelessness, delusion (read: 14.13 [BG]); it "covers knowledge" (*jñānam āvṛtya*) (read: 14.9 [BG]).
- *mūḍham layaṃ jaḍam* (gloss: "bewildered, dissolving, dull") (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.15 [BP]); "one's higher awareness fails … his mind is ruined … depression" (read: 11.25.18 [BP]).
- Food of tamas: *yāta-yāmaṃ gata-rasaṃ pūti paryuṣitaṃ ca yat | ucchiṣṭam api cāmedhyam* (gloss: "stale, tasteless, putrid, kept overnight, leavings, impure") (read: Gītā 17.10 [BG]). Bhāgavata: "unclean and causes distress" (read: 11.25.28 [BP]).
- Gītā 18.22 (knowledge clinging to one thing as if it were the whole); 18.25 (action begun from delusion); 18.28 (the doer "unsteady, vulgar, stubborn, deceitful, malicious, lazy, despondent, procrastinating") (read [BG]).

**Temperament and feeling named.**
- Ignorance (*ajñāna*) as the fruit (read: Gītā 14.16 [BG]); delusion and carelessness from tamas (14.17 [BG]).
- Qualities in Bhāgavata 11.25.2–5: "intolerant anger, stinginess … living as a parasite, hypocrisy, chronic fatigue, quarrel, lamentation, delusion … sleeping too much … fear and laziness" (read [BP]); deep, dreamless sleep (*prasvāpa*) from tamas (11.25.20 [BP]); residence in a gambling house (11.25.25 [BP]).
- Caraka lists three tamas types: paśava (animals, "devoid of intelligence, … indulged in sexual act and sleep"), matsya (fish: "timid, unwise, gluttonous, unstable … always moving and liking water"), vānaspatya (plant: "idle, indulged only in food and devoid of … intelligence") (read: Śārīra 4.39–40 [CS]).
- Gītā 17.13, 17.19, 18.32, 18.35, 18.39 (the tāmasa offering without rite or faith; tapas by foolish self-torment; the intellect that takes wrong for right, "covered by darkness" *tamasāvṛtā*; the "dull-witted" hold that does not give up sleep, fear, grief, despair, pride; happiness that "deludes the self" and comes from sleep, sloth and carelessness) (read [BG]).
- The most wicked (*rakṣasāṃ*) strengthen with tamas (read: Bhāgavata 11.25.19 [BP]).

**The three together.** Sāṃkhya Kārikā 12: they are "of the nature of pleasure, pain and indifference", and function by "suppression, co-operation, transformation and intimate intercourse" (*anyonyābhibhavāśraya-janana-mithuna-vṛttayaś ca*) (read [SK]). The editor quotes the Devī Bhāgavata on one being in the dominant and two recessive in turn (read [SK], note). Bhāgavata 11.25.30 says that substance, place, time, knowledge, action, doer, faith, state of consciousness and destination "are all based on the three modes" (read [BP]). The Yogacūḍāmaṇi gives the three lords as Brahmā (rājasa), Viṣṇu (sāttvika) and Rudra (tāmasa) in one passage, and as the sounds *a*, *u*, *m* in another (read: Yogacūḍāmaṇi, verse 72 and verse 75 as printed [YC]). The lords of the five bhūtas there are named *Sadāśiva, Īśvara, Rudra, Viṣṇu, Brahmā* (read: [YC]); the Gheraṇḍa gives the same deities at the dhāraṇās: Brahmā for earth, Viṣṇu for water, Rudra for fire, Īśvara for air, Sadāśiva for space (read: Gheraṇḍa 3.70–80 [GhV], [GhM], [GhS]).

**Gross elements as sattva, rajas, tamas.** Sāṃkhya Kārikā 38 says the gross elements are *śānta* (tranquil), *ghora* (terrific) and *mūḍha* (delusive), "according to the prominent constituent — Sattva, Rajas or Tamas" (the editor's note), without naming which gross element is which (read [SK]).

---

| Vāyu (air) | black as collyrium (read: Gheraṇḍa 3.77 [GhV]; Śāradā 1.21 [ST]); smoke-coloured (read: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.22 [SC]); sky-blue (read: Svarodaya v.151 [SV]) | circle (read: Gheraṇḍa [GhM]); hexagonal (read: Yogatattva [YT]; Ṣaṭcakra v.22 [SC]); circle with six dots (read: Śāradā 1.23 [ST]); spherical (read: Svarodaya v.149 [SV]) | between the eyebrows (read [GhM]); heart to eyebrows (read [YT]); north; flows at acute angles (read: Svarodaya v.150, 164, 171 [SV]); ascends (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]) | flow of eight fingers (read: Svarodaya v.155 [SV]) | light, cold, rough, coarse, non-slimy, minute (read: Caraka Sū. 26.11 [CS]); mobility (read: Caraka Śā. 1.29–30 [CS]); hot or cool (read: Svarodaya v.164 [SV]) | touch (sparśa) (read: Caraka Śā. 1.27 [CS]); acid (read: Svarodaya v.154 [SV]) | collyrium, lampblack; black antelope in the centre (read: Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa v.22 [SC]) | "full of sattva" (read: Gheraṇḍa 3.77 [GhV]); restlessness; "killing" acts (read: Svarodaya v.156–157 [SV]) |
| Ākāśa (space) | pure sea-water (read: Gheraṇḍa 3.80 [GhV]); white (read: Ṣaṭcakra v.28–29 [SC]); smoky (read: Yogatattva [YT]); all colours foreshadowed (read: Svarodaya v.151 [SV]); svaccha (read: Śāradā 1.21 [ST]) | none given (read: Gheraṇḍa [GhV], [GhM]); circle (read: Yogatattva [YT]; Śāradā 1.23 [ST]; Ṣaṭcakra [SC]); spotted, ear-shaped, formless (read: Svarodaya v.149, 169, 207 [SV]) | aperture of Brahman (read [GhM]); eyebrows to top of head (read [YT]); the middle; moving everywhere (read: Svarodaya v.150, 169, 171 [SV]) | | soft, light, minute, smooth (read: Caraka Sū. 26.11 [CS]); non-interruption (read: Caraka Śā. 1.29–30 [CS]); porous, pliant (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]) | sound (śabda) (read: Caraka Śā. 1.27 [CS]); bitter (read: Svarodaya v.154 [SV]); no definite taste (read: Suśruta 41 [SS]) | "clearest water" (read: Gheraṇḍa [GhM]); pure crystal for Sadāśiva (read: Yogatattva [YT]) | opens the gates of emancipation (read: Gheraṇḍa 3.80 [GhV]); only for Yoga (read: Svarodaya v.158, 165 [SV]) |

#### What I could not find

- **Śiva Svarodaya in Sanskrit.** I did not obtain the Sanskrit verses of the Svarodaya. The tattva colours, shapes, tastes, directions and measures above are from Rama Prasad's English (his numbering, verses 141–207), plus a modern English introduction with a damaged table. His own commentary, which gives air as "green" and ākāśa as "dark", is separate from his translation of the verses, which give air "sky-blue" and ākāśa "foreshadows every colour". The printed bīja letters in his translation of verses 203–207 are partly garbled in the scan, so I give none from it.
- **Sources blocked.** The pages on sacred-texts.com, sanskritdocuments.org and wisdomlib.org refused automated access when I tried; I used archive.org, Wikisource, Vedabase and a few other sites instead.
- **Gheraṇḍa Saṃhitā numbering and Sanskrit.** The editions opened give the five dhāraṇās at 3.70–80 (not 3.69–77). The Sanskrit in the Vasu scan is unreadable; Mallinson's Sanskrit is printed as images in the PDF I opened. The Sanskrit I read is a vulgate transcription; some words in it look misprinted (the fire verse prints *mandra-gopa* where both translators read Indragopa; the earth verse prints *vedāstaṃ* where the translators read "four-sided"), so I used it for the colour words and bījas only.
- **Caraka and Suśruta Sanskrit.** The Caraka scan (Sharma) and the Suśruta scan (Bhishagratna) are English-readable but the Sanskrit lines are unreadable. I did not open the Cakrapāṇi or Ḍalhaṇa commentaries. Caraka Sūtra 1 (the list of the twenty guṇas) was read only in the translator's footnote; the footnote lists the pairs *guru-laghu, śīta-uṣṇa, snigdha-rūkṣa, manda-tīkṣṇa, ślakṣṇa-khara, sāndra-drava, mṛdu-kaṭhina, sthira-sara, sūkṣma-sthūla, viśada-picchila*, and I did not map them to the bhūtas beyond what Sūtra 26.11 says.
- **Vaiśeṣika Sūtra.** I read the sūtras only as quoted in an English translation with the Upaskāra commentary, whose scan is partly illegible (the sūtra on air). I did not open the Praśastapāda Bhāṣya, which gives the full colour and touch list for each element.
- **Śāradā Tilaka.** Only verses 19–24 of paṭala 1 (with Rāghavabhaṭṭa's comment) were read. I did not read the later paṭalas (bhūta-śuddhi, yoga) and I did not open the Viśvasāra Tantra or the Prapañcasāra Tantra; those are cited only as Woodroffe reports them.
- **Metal and gem.** No passage I read ties a bhūta to a named metal or gem. What I found: orpiment (a mineral) for earth in the Gheraṇḍa; "colour as pure as gold" in a meditation on earth (Svarodaya v.203); "pure crystal" for two deities (Yogatattva); and a gem-lustre derivation for the Maṇipūra lotus (Ṣaṭcakranirūpaṇa, via Woodroffe). I did not search the Ratnaśāstra or the Rasaśāstra literature.
- **Purāṇas.** Bhāgavata 11.25 gives no colour for the guṇas; its list of marks (knowledge, action, abode, faith, food, happiness) is above. I found no Purāṇic verse naming a colour for sattva, rajas or tamas. The only guṇa colours I opened are in the Yogacūḍāmaṇi Upaniṣad. I did not open the Mahābhārata (the Anugītā's guṇa passages, and the varṇa-colour passage of the Śānti Parvan), the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa or Manu 12.24–52.
- **Chāndogya and Śvetāśvatara Sanskrit.** I read these in Müller's English and notes only; I did not open the Sanskrit lines for 6.4 or 4.5.
- **Bṛhat Saṃhitā and the Vāstu texts.** I found no passage giving forms to the five elements. Chapter 52 (as numbered on Wikisource) names soil colours for the four varṇas and the smell and taste of the site; chapter 53 on groundwater signs lists soil colours and animals, and was not used. I did not open the Mayamata, the Mānasāra or the Samarāṅgaṇasūtradhāra.
- **Yogacūḍāmaṇi.** The text gives no colour or shape for the five bhūtas; its only colour passage is the praṇava one above.
- **Size, thickness and lustre of the guṇas.** No text I read gives these; a guṇa's size is not spoken of. For the five bhūtas, thickness is spoken of only in Caraka and Suśruta (above).
- **Sāṃkhya Kārikā 38 (śānta, ghora, mūḍha).** The verse does not say which gross element is tranquil, terrific or delusive; the commentary I read gives the guṇas as the cause without a table.

## Part Two: Later authors

### Planets, signs and elements tied to colour, shape, line and form: Western sources, read

A gathering of what Western authors actually wrote, from antiquity to the twentieth century, when they tied the planets, the zodiac signs and the four elements to colour, shape, line, form, metal, direction and talisman design; together with authors who tie colour to shape and feeling in design with no astrology at all.

Terms. I use "planet" for what Sanskrit calls a graha (graha, "seizer", the nine bodies of Indian astrology). Where a Western text speaks of the two lunar nodes (Rāhu and Ketu in Sanskrit usage), it calls them the Head and the Tail of the Dragon. The "seven planets" of the Western tradition are Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus and Saturn.

#### How to read this part

- "(read: URL)" means I opened the page or text at that address and read the passage. "(read)" inside a block means the same, at the URL given in that block's heading.
- "(memory)" means the statement comes from my recollection, or from a search-result summary whose page I did not open. Nothing marked "(memory)" should be taken as checked.
- Several texts came to me as scanned text (OCR) from archive.org. Where the scan was garbled I say so, and I give only what was legible.
- Quotations are as the edition I opened prints them, including old spelling. Square brackets are the editors' or mine.
- Whether astrology is in the source is stated for each author as "Astrology: yes / no / partly".

#### Section 1. Ancient and medieval sources

##### Plato, Republic X (the Myth of Er) and Timaeus

Astrology: not astrology, but the earliest Greek statement of planet colours and of shapes for the elements that later authors cite.

Editions opened: Benjamin Jowett's translation, in the 1901 Colonial Press volume of the Republic, https://archive.org/details/republicofplatoi00platiala (Republic X, pp. 324-325 of that volume); Jowett's Timaeus at https://classics.mit.edu/Plato/timaeus.html.

- Republic X, the spindle of Necessity (the eight whorls are the heavens): "The largest (or fixed stars) is spangled, and the seventh (or sun) is brightest; the eighth (or moon) colored by the reflected light of the seventh; the second and fifth (Saturn and Mercury) are in color like one another, and yellower than the preceding; the third (Venus) has the whitest light; the fourth (Mars) is reddish; the sixth (Jupiter) is in whiteness second." The planet names in parentheses are the translator's. (read)
- Timaeus, the four elements as solids built of triangles: the first body, of four equilateral triangles, is the pyramid; the "cube, having six plane quadrangular equilateral bases" comes from the isosceles triangle; a third is "made up of 120 triangular elements, forming twelve solid angles ... twenty bases". Then: "To earth, then, let us assign the cubical form; for earth is the most immoveable of the four"; water gets "the least moveable" of the remaining forms, fire the most moveable, air the intermediate; "the pyramid is the solid which is the original element and seed of fire". The passage says "There was yet a fifth combination which God used in the delineation of the universe" (the dodecahedron, not named in this sentence). (read: https://classics.mit.edu/Plato/timaeus.html)
- Timaeus on fire's heat: "the fineness of the sides, and the sharpness of the angles, and the smallness of the particles, and the swiftness of the motion" make fire cut; "that which rests on quadrangular bases is firmly posed" and is hard. A link from angle sharpness to a felt quality is stated here, with no planet. (read: same URL)

##### Ptolemy, Tetrabiblos (c. 150 CE)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: Loeb Classical Library, F. E. Robbins, 1940, as transcribed by Bill Thayer at https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Ptolemy/Tetrabiblos/1B*.html (Book I, chapters 4 to 7; page markers p37 to p45) and .../3C*.html (Book III, chapter 11, Loeb pp. 309-317).

- I.4 gives each planet an active quality: Sun "heating and, to a certain degree, drying"; Moon mostly humidifying; Saturn "chiefly to cool and, moderately, to dry"; Mars "chiefly to dry and to burn, in conformity with his fiery colour and by reason of his nearness to the sun"; Jupiter temperate, "both heats and humidifies"; Venus "the same powers and tempered nature as Jupiter" but chiefly humidifying; Mercury drying and humidifying by turns. (read)
- The only planet colour in I.4 is Mars: "his fiery colour". I found no colour listed for any other planet in I.4. (read)
- I.5 sorts planets as beneficent (Jupiter, Venus, the Moon: hot and moist) and maleficent (Saturn: excessive cold; Mars: excessive dryness); Sun and Mercury have both powers. I.6: Moon and Venus feminine, Sun, Saturn, Jupiter and Mars masculine, Mercury common. (read)
- III.11, "Of Bodily Form and Temperament", gives each planet a body. Saturn rising: "dark-skinned, robust, black-haired, curly-haired, hairy-chested, with eyes of moderate size, of middling stature". Saturn setting: "dark, slender, small, straight-haired ... black-eyed". Jupiter rising: "light of skin, but in such a way as to have a good colour, with moderately curling hair and large eyes, tall". Mars rising: "red and white of complexion, tall and robust, gray-eyed". Venus: "more shapely, graceful, womanish, effeminate in figure, plump, and luxurious". Mercury rising: "sallow, of moderate height, graceful, with small eyes". (read: https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/E/Roman/Texts/Ptolemy/Tetrabiblos/3C*.html)
- III.11 also ties signs to bodily shape: "Leo, Virgo, and Sagittarius make them larger; others, as Pisces, Cancer, and Capricorn, smaller"; the fore parts of Aries, Taurus and Leo make the upper body robust; Virgo, Libra and Sagittarius tend to make people "well-proportioned and graceful", while "Scorpio, Pisces, and Taurus bring about awkwardness and disproportion". It also ties each quarter of the year to a complexion (spring quarter "well-favoured in complexion"; the quarter from winter solstice to spring equinox "dark complexion ... straight hair"). (read: same URL)

##### Vettius Valens, Anthologies (2nd century CE)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: Mark Riley's English translation as reformatted and annotated at https://github.com/janegca/latex-valens, raw files https://raw.githubusercontent.com/janegca/latex-valens/master/book01/01-stars.tex and .../02-signs.tex. This is a re-typeset copy of Riley's unfinished draft; I did not open Riley's own PDF (its address returned an error). Treat wording as Riley's, layout as the copyist's.

- Book I, section 1, "The Nature of the Stars", gives each planet a colour and a taste: Sun "yellowish", bitter; Moon "green in color", salty; Saturn "like castor in color", astringent; Jupiter "gray verging on white", sweet; Mars "red in color", acid; Venus "white in color", very greasy. For Mercury the line is damaged in the Greek, and the translator supplies in angle brackets "<It is blue in color, sharp in taste.>". (read: raw URL for 01-stars.tex)
- The same section assigns materials: Sun gold; Moon silver and glass; Saturn lead, wood and stone; Jupiter tin; Mars iron; Venus precious stones and fancy jewelry; Mercury copper and coins. (read)
- Book I, section 2, describes each sign by form. Aries is "two-toned (since the Sun and the Moon make white lichen)". Virgo is "winged, anthropomorphic ... standing like the figure of Justice". Libra and Aquarius are "anthropomorphic". Scorpio has "many feet". Capricorn is "a hunchback, lame". Pisces is "moist, quite wet, bicorporeal ... mossy, scaley, sinewy, humpbacked, leprous, two-formed". Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius and Pisces are "bicorporeal". (read: raw URL for 02-signs.tex)

##### Hephaistio of Thebes, Apotelesmatics (c. 415 CE)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: Robert Schmidt's translation, edited by Robert Hand, Project Hindsight Greek Track vol. VI, Book I, scanned text at https://archive.org/stream/livingsignsii/Hephaistio%20of%20Thebes%20-%20Apotelesmatics,%20Book%20I_djvu.txt

- I.24, "Concerning the Colors in Eclipses and Comets": "when they appear dark or sallow, it becomes indicative of ... the nature of Kronos; when white, of ... Zeus; reddish, for the nature of Ares; yellow, for the nature of Aphrodite; multi-colored, for the nature of Hermes." (Kronos, Zeus, Ares, Aphrodite, Hermes are Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Venus, Mercury.) (read)
- The same chapter describes a comet "of Kronos" as "round and has an amber color similar to Kronos, and is amorphous", with rays "scattered around its periphery"; a comet "of Zeus" is "silvery". (read)
- I.3 says the power of fixed stars is "taken according to the similarity to the planets of the powers and natures suggested by color, as the ancients and the divine Ptolemy have laid out." (read)

##### Al-Biruni, Book of Instruction in the Elements of the Art of Astrology (1029)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: R. Ramsay Wright, 1934 (Arabic facsimile and English), scanned text at https://archive.org/download/albirunibookofinstruction/albiruni_djvu.txt. The scan is poor; planet names and many words are damaged. I give only what was legible. Paragraph numbers are Biruni's, as Wright prints them.

- §§396-401, "Natures of the planets and their indications": each planet gets elementary qualities, benefic or malefic, sex, sect, smell and taste, and colour. Saturn: "extremely cold and dry ... Jet-black also black mixed with yellow, lead colour, pitch-dark." Jupiter: "Dust-colour and white mixed with yellow and brown, shining, glittering." Mars: "Dark red." Sun: "shining reddish-yellow, its colour is said to be that of the lord of the hour." Venus: "Pure white tending to straw-colour, shining, according to some greenish." Mercury: "Complex flavour and colour, the latter sky-blue mixed with a darker colour." Moon: "Blue and white or some deep colour not unmixed with reddish yellow, moderate brilliancy." (read)
- §§402-406, which Wright's heading (partly legible) describes as qualities and forms, and the days, climates and soils: Mars "Hot, hard, sharp and red things. Length, dryness and coarseness"; Venus "Squareness, dispersion, smoothness"; Jupiter "Moderation, solidity, smoothness"; Moon "Density, moisture, opacity, lightness"; Saturn "Shortness, dryness, ... heaviness" (one word garbled in the scan). The Sun's entry begins "Revolution". (read)
- §389, relation to the points of the compass: Biruni says he has not seen the planets tied to directions in ordinary books except in Nayrizi, "who in speaking of the four triplicities refers Saturn to the East, Mars to the West, Venus to the South and Jupiter to the North". He then reports a Hindu system and concludes "the East with Mercury and Jupiter, the West with Saturn, the South with the sun and Mars and the North with Venus and the moon". (read)
- §427-428, "Figure and face": each planet governs kin and a bodily look. Saturn: "Ugly, tall, wizened, sour face, large head, eyebrows joined, small eyes, wide mouth, thick lips ... short neck, coarse hand, short fingers, awkward figure, legs crooked, big feet." Jupiter: "Fine figure, round face, thick prominent nose, large eyes, frank look, small beard, abundant curly hair reddish." Mars: "Tall, large head, small eyes and ears ... lank hair, reddish, long fingers, long steps." Sun: "Large head, complexion white inclining to yellow, long hair". Venus: "Fine round face, reddish-white complexion, double chin ... medium tall, short fingers, thick calves." Mercury: "complexion brown with a greenish tinge ... narrow forehead". Moon: "Clear white complexion, gait and figure erect, round face". (read)
- §449-450: the "faces" (ten-degree thirds of a sign) are called "figures" because "the Greeks, Hindus and Babylonians associated with each face as it arose the figure of a personage human or divine". (read)
- §362-364 has a table of the signs with colours, trades and classes. The scan of that table is too damaged to read as a table; I saw only fragments such as "Colours mixed ... peacock, brown and green. Piebald, black and white" and could not assign them to signs. (not read)

##### Abu Ma'shar, Dorotheus, Firmicus Maternus, Ibn Ezra

I did not open the text of any of these for colours or shapes. See "What I could not find or open".

#### Section 2. Renaissance and early modern sources

##### Marsilio Ficino, De vita coelitus comparanda (Book III of De vita libri tres, 1489)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: Carol V. Kaske and John R. Clark, Three Books on Life (1989, Latin and English), scanned text at https://archive.org/download/marsilio-ficino-three-books-on-life/Marsilio%20Ficino%20-%20Three%20Books%20on%20Life%20%281989%29_djvu.txt

- III.11 (English p. 297): "Colors which are very watery, white, green, a bit yellowish, similar to the colors of violets, roses, and lilies ... refer to Venus, the Moon, and Mercury. But the colors of sapphire, which also are said to be airy, and richer purple colors, golden mixed with silver, and greens, always refer to Jupiter. But blazing yellows, pure golds, and lighter purple colors refer to the Sun." Also: any colours "if they are fresh or at least pertain to silk, are more stellar; they are potent also in metals and stones and glass because of their resemblance to celestial things." (read)
- III.19 (about pp. 345-347), on making a small model of the world: it should have "not only lines but colors". "There are, indeed, three colors of the world ... green, gold, and sapphire-blue, dedicated to the three heavenly Graces. Green is the color of Venus and also of the Moon ... gold is the color of the Sun, and besides not alien to Jupiter and Venus. Finally, sapphire-blue we especially dedicate to Jupiter." Gold stars on the spheres; the earth ("Vesta ... or Ceres") clothed in a green garment; a vaulted chamber "marked with these figures and colors" in which to spend most waking hours. (read)
- III.17 (English p. 331): "colors are particular lights. On which account (so astrologers say), you should not rashly deny their statement that lights — that is, colors, figures, and numbers — can do a great deal towards preparing our materials for celestial things." (read)

##### Cornelius Agrippa, De occulta philosophia / Three Books of Occult Philosophy (1533)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: the 1651 English translation (J. F.), as transcribed with Latin collation notes by Joseph H. Peterson at https://www.esotericarchives.com/agrippa/op1.htm (Book I) and https://www.esotericarchives.com/agrippa/op2.htm (Book II). The chapter numbers below are that edition's.

Book I, the natural world:

- I.8: "some [stars] are fiery, as Mars, and Sol; airy, as Jupiter, and Venus: watery, as Saturn, and Mercury: and earthy, such as inhabit the eighth Orbe, and the Moon (which notwithstanding by many is accounted watery)". In the signs, each element has "beginning, middle, and end": Aries, Leo, Sagittarius for fire; Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn for earth; Gemini, Libra, Aquarius for air; Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces for water. (read: op1.htm)
- I.24 to I.29, things under each planet, give elements, metals, stones and colours: Moon, "amongst Elements, ... the Earth, then the Water", "amongst Metals, Silver", stones "that are White, and Green". Saturn, "Earth, and also Water", "Lead, and Gold", "all dark, weighty, earthy things". Jupiter, "the Aire", "Tin, Silver, and Gold", stones including sapphire and emerald and "[permanently green and copper coloured] aiery colours". Mars, "Fire", "Iron, and red Brass; and all fiery, red, and sulphureous things". Venus, "Aire, and Water", "Silver, and Brass, both yellow, and red", "all of a fair, various, white, and green Colour". Mercury, "Water", "Quick-silver, Tin", stones "of divers colours, and various figures naturally ... those which have a colour mixed with yellow, and green". (read: op1.htm)
- I.30: "every thing that bears Flowers is from Venus, all Seed, and Bark is from Mercury, and all roots from Saturne, and all Wood from Mars, and leaves from the Moon"; in stones "their hardness from Mars ... their beauty and fairness from Venus". (read)
- I.49, "Of Light, Colours, Candles, and Lamps, and to what Stars, Houses, and Elements severall colours are ascribed". Colours "are a kind of lights, which being mixed with things, are wont to expose them to those Stars, to which they are agreeable." The list: Saturn: "black, lucid, earthy, leaden" (the editor notes the Latin does not say brown); Jupiter: "Saphire, and airy colours, and those which are alwaies green, clear, purple, darkish, golden, mixed with Silver"; Mars: "Red colours, and burning, fiery, flaming, violet, purple, bloody, and iron colours"; Sun: "Golden, Saffron, purple, and bright colours"; "Venus, Mercury, and the Moon": "all white, fair, curious, green, ruddy, betwixt saffron, and purple". (read: op1.htm)
- I.49 continues with twelve "houses of the heaven" (the transcriber glosses "signes of the zodiack"): "the first and seventh hath white colour: the second, and twelfth green: the third, and eleventh saffron: the fourth, and the tenth red: the fift, and ninth honey colour: the sixt, and eighth, black." The four elements have humour-colours: "an earthy colour, caused of coldness, and dryness is brown, and black, and manifests black Choller, and a Saturnine nature; the blew tending towards whiteness, doth denote flegme ... reddish colour shews blood, but fiery, flaming, burning hot, shew choller". (read)

Book II, number, figure and image:

- II.21, numbers and elements (Pythagoreans): "to the Aire they have assigned the number eight, and to fire five, to the earth six, to the water twelve." Planet numbers: unity to the Sun (and Jupiter); two to the Moon, Saturn and Mars; three to Jupiter, Sun, Venus; four to the Sun; five to Mercury; six to Venus; seven to Saturn and the Moon; eight to Jupiter; nine to the Moon and Mars; ten and twelve to the Sun; eleven to the Moon. (read: op2.htm)
- II.22, "Of the tables of the Planets": seven magic squares, one per planet: Saturn a square of three (nine cells, "in every line three every way, and through each Diameter making fifteen", total 45), engraved on a plate of lead; Jupiter four by four (sum 34, total 136), silver plate, coral; Mars five by five (the transcription prints "a square of four containing twenty five numbers"; line sum 65, total 325), iron plate or sword, carnelian, red brass if unfortunate; Sun six by six (111, total 666), gold plate; Venus seven by seven (175, total 1225), silver plate, brass if unfortunate; Mercury eight by eight (260, total 2080), silver, tin, yellow brass or virgin parchment; Moon nine by nine (369, total 3321), silver, lead if unfortunate. From each square "is drawn the seal, or Character of Saturn, and of the spirits thereof". The seal drawings themselves are figures that the text does not describe in words. (read: op2.htm)
- II.23, "Of Geometrical Figures and Bodies": "a Circle doth answer to Unity and the number ten ... a circular being the largest and perfectest of all is judged to be the most fit for bindings and conjurations; Whence they who adjure evil spirits, are wont to environ themselves about with a circle." The pentangle "hath a very great command over evil spirits", "within five obtuse angles, and without five acutes". The cross: "the rightest figure of all, containing foure right angles ... the most firm receptacle of all Celestial powers". Then the five solids by Pythagoras and Plato: to earth "a four square, and a square of eight solid angles ... six bases in form of a Dice"; to fire "a Pryamis of a four triangular basis"; to air "Octocedron, of eight triangular bases"; to water "Icocedron twenty bases"; to the heaven "Dodecacedron of twelve five cornered bases". I read the whole chapter: it assigns figures to the elements and the heaven, and to no individual planet. (read: op2.htm)
- II.36, images of the zodiac: Aries, Leo, Sagittarius "the fiery and oriental triplicity"; Gemini, Libra, Aquarius "the Aerial and Occidental Triplicity"; Cancer, Scorpio, Pisces "the watry & Northern Triplicity"; Taurus, Virgo, Capricorn "the earthly and Southern triplicity". Direction thus runs fire east, air west, water north, earth south. (read)
- II.38 to II.44, talisman figures per planet, with stone, hour and dress. Saturn (II.38): an old man "setting upon an high chayre ... his head covered with a black or dusky coloured cloth, and all his garments black or dark coloured", with sickle or fish; or an old man "leaning on a staff ... cloathed in black"; also "the Image of a man, having the countenance of an Hart, and Camels feet ... sitting upon a Chayr or Dragon, holding in his right hand, a sithe". Jupiter (II.39): a crowned man "cloathed with garments of a Saffron Colour, riding upon an Eagle or Dragon", in "a clear and white stone, especially in Crystall"; or a man with a lion's or ram's head. Mars (II.40): an armed man riding a lion with a naked sword and a man's head, "in a Martiall stone, especially in a Diamond". Sun (II.41): a crowned king in a chair "cloathed in Saffron coloured cloathes ... under his feet a Globe", in "a Balanite stone or a Rubin"; or a woman in a chariot of four horses "carrying a flame of fire on her head". Venus (II.42): a woman with a bird's head; or a naked maid with a mirror, in lapis lazuli; or "a little maide with her hair spread abroad, cloathed in long and white garments, holding a Laurell Apple, or flowes in her right hand, in her left a Combe". Mercury (II.43): a bearded young man with a serpent-twined rod and winged feet; or a man on a peacock. Moon (II.44): a man with a staff and a bird; or "a woman cornuted, riding on a Bull, or a Dragon with seven heads, or a Crab ... clothed in white or green". (read: op2.htm)
- II.45, the nodes: for the Head and Tail of the Dragon "betwixt an Aeriall and fiery circle, the likeness of a Serpent, with the head of an Hawke tyed about them, after the manner of the great letter Theta"; the head image for good fortune, the tail image made "to introduce, anguish, infirmity and misfortune". (read: op2.htm)

##### Paracelsus, Archidoxis Magica, Book II (R. Turner's English, 1656)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: the 1656 English of the second treatise, "Of Celestial Medicines, Containing, The Mysteries of the Signes of the Zodiack", scanned at https://archive.org/download/paracelsus-archidoxis-magicae/Paracelsus%20-%20Archidoxis%20Magicae_djvu.txt (about fifteen kilobytes; a short extract, and the seal drawings are lost in the scan).

- The treatise gives a seal for each of the twelve signs, with its metal and timing. Aries is cast of mixed metals, applied "when Mars is in the Ninth House"; Cancer "of most pure Silver"; Leo "of pure Gold"; Libra "of pure" copper (the metal symbol is garbled in the scan, and Venus is named as ruler); Scorpio "of pure Iron in the day and hour of Mars"; Sagittarius "of pure Tyrne [tin] ... in the hour of Jupiter"; Capricorn "of Gold; for Lead hath no operation with other Metals", with a copper ring; Aquarius of gold, lead and iron; Pisces of gold, iron, copper, silver and tin. (read)
- The text says the seal "is to be engraven as this Figure" and gives drawings for the signs, such as a scorpion image for Scorpio; the figures did not survive in the scan, so I cannot say what shapes they take. (read)

##### William Lilly, Christian Astrology (1647)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: the 1647 first edition scanned at https://archive.org/download/ca-william-lilly/CA_William_Lilly__djvu.txt (long-s scan; the page headers read "An Introduction to Astrologie", Book I). The scan is rough; the passages below were legible.

- Book I, p. 86, a table headed "The colours of the Planets, and Signes": Saturn "black colour"; Jupiter "a colour mixed with red and greene"; Mars "red, or iron colour"; Sun "yellow or yellow. Purple" (the scan prints the full stop; probably "yellow or yellow-purple"); Venus "white or purple colour"; Mercury "sky-colour or blewish"; Moon "a colour spotted with white and other mixt colours". Signs, in order: Aries "White mixed with red"; Taurus "white mixed with Citrine"; Gemini "white mixed with red"; Cancer "greene or ruffet"; Leo "red or green"; Virgo "blacke speckled with blew"; Libra "black or darke crimson, or tawny"; Scorpio "browne"; Sagittarius "yellow or a greene sanguine"; Capricorn "blacks or ruffet, or a swart browne"; Aquarius "a skye-colour with blew"; Pisces "white glistering colour". (read; the planet and sign glyphs in the scan are garbled, but twelve sign entries and seven planet entries stand in the expected order)
- Book I, planet chapters, the planet seen in the sky: Saturn is "not very bright ... but is of a Pale, Wan Colour or Leaden, Ashy colour"; Jupiter "in his Colour he is bright, cleer, and of an Azure colour"; Mars "appeareth to our fight of a shining, fiery, sparkling colour"; Venus "of a bright shining colour". (read)
- Book I, Saturn's body ("Corporature"): "spare, leane and not long ... his Complexion pale, swartish or muddy, his Eyes little and black, looking downward, a broad Forehead, black or sad Haire ... a lumpish, unpleasant Countenance". Saturn rules lead, the lodestone, "Saphire, Lapis Lazuli, all black, ugly Country Stones not polishable, and of a sad, ashy or black colour". (read)
- Book I, the people the planets signify: Mars "full faced with a lively high colour like Sunne-burnt ... his eyes being sparkling or sharpe and darting, and of yellow colour; his haire ... reddish", varying with the sign; Sun "an obscure white colour mixed with red; a round face, and short chin ... his hayre curling"; Venus "a goodly and faire round visage ... the body extreame well shaped, ever rather inclining to shortnesse then talnesse"; Mercury "neither white or black but betwixt both, of a sad brown or dark yellowish color, long visage, high forehead ... a thin long sharpe nose ... slender of body, small legs". (read)
- Lilly also gives colours to houses, in his house descriptions: the sixth house "black colour" (Saturn "rejoyceth in this house"); the seventh "a darke Blacke colour"; the eighth "the Green and Black"; the eleventh "the Saffron or Yellow". I did not read all twelve. Agrippa's pairs (above) have black for the sixth and eighth and saffron for the eleventh; Lilly agrees there and differs at the seventh (Agrippa: white). (read)

#### Section 3. Modern astrological and occult authors

##### Alan Leo, Esoteric Astrology (1913)

Astrology: yes (Theosophical).

Edition opened: scanned text at https://archive.org/download/alan-leo-esoteric-astrology-1913/Alan%20Leo%20-%20Esoteric%20Astrology%20%281913%29_djvu.txt (chapter I, "Astrological Symbology", pp. 3-7). The scan drops the glyph drawings, so I read the words.

- Glyph parts: "From these three glyphs of circle, semi-circle, and cross the symbols of the astrological planets may be derived". The cross "stands for Body, as distinguished from the circle, Spirit, and the semi-circle, Soul." (read)
- Planet by planet: Sun "Unity. Life or Consciousness. The individual self. Spirit"; Moon "Duality, relationship. The formative principle. The personal self. Soul"; Earth (cross in circle) "Activity in matter. The material self. Body"; Venus "The spiritual self or individuality, risen above matter"; Mars "Matter dominating spirit"; Jupiter "The soul expanding beyond matter, but retaining the material form"; Saturn "The concrete soul, limited by material conditions"; Mercury "The cross below signifies astral consciousness, desire; the circle in the middle, mental consciousness; the semi-circle above indicates that evolution has been pushed beyond the mental"; Mercury's glyph "may also be interpreted as the caduceus of Hermes". (read)
- Colour, ch. I, p. 7: "the white light of the Sun is changed on passing through the planetary sphere into the colour of that particular planet, the indigo, violet, and blue of Venus, Moon, and Jupiter, having their reflection in the yellow, orange, and red of Mercury, Sun, and Mars, balanced by the green of Saturn, the middle note and colour." (read)
- p. 66-67: "The tones and colours are related to the triple arrangements of signs, primarily in the Cardinal, Fixed and Mutable signs, producing violet, indigo, and blue, respectively, with green as the middle colour; and these, reversed again ... produce the Mutable, Fixed and Cardinal triplicities, yellow, orange and red." A table follows giving each sign a "characteristic", colour and musical tone; in the scan the sign symbols are garbled, so I could not assign colours to individual signs. (read, partly)

##### Aleister Crowley, 777 (1909; 1955 revised), after the Golden Dawn scales

Astrology: yes (Hermetic Qabalah).

Edition opened: 777 Revised, https://archive.org/download/Liber777Revised/Liber777revised_djvu.txt. Columns XV to XVIII are the King, Queen, Emperor and Empress scales of colour. The tables came through the scan in broken columns; I cross-checked the planet and sign rows against the author's own explanatory prose, and the cross-check agreed except where noted.

- King scale, planets (Column XV): Mercury yellow, Moon blue, Venus emerald (the prose says "green"), Jupiter violet, Mars scarlet, Sun orange, Saturn indigo. Prose: "Saturn whose indigo represents the sobriety and deep-sea calmness of meditation ... Luna is blue; representing purity, aspiration and unselfish love. The green of Venus suggests the vibration of vegetable growth ... The yellow of Mercury suggests the balanced but articulate movement of the mind. The orange of Sol is the intense but gross physical vibration of animal life." The violet of Jupiter is "religious and creative", the red of Mars "physical, violent and gross"; the planetary colours "follow the colours of the spectrum". (read; pp. 76-77)
- Queen scale, planets (Column XVI): "The planetary colours are connected chiefly with the sacred metals". Mercury purple, "the iridescence of quicksilver"; Luna silver; Venus sky-blue; Jupiter blue; Mars red, "on account of the colour of rust, and of the use of iron"; Sol yellow, "the metal gold"; Saturn black, "with reference to Binah ... the blackness of oblivion (Saturn being Time)". (read; pp. 80-81)
- King scale, signs: "The Zodiacal colours proceed systematically from the Scarlet of Aries to the violet of Aquarius." Aries scarlet; Taurus red-orange; Gemini orange; Cancer amber; Leo "pure yellow, yet with that tinge of green"; Virgo "yellowish green of young grass"; Libra emerald; Scorpio "greenish blue — Prussian blue"; Sagittarius blue; Capricorn indigo; Aquarius violet; Pisces crimson. (read; pp. 77-79)
- Queen scale, signs, as read from the table (column alignment checked against the prose for Aries red, Taurus deep indigo, Capricorn black; the prose calls Virgo "slate-green" where the table scan reads "slate grey"): Aries red; Taurus deep indigo; Gemini pale mauve; Cancer maroon; Leo deep purple; Virgo slate grey; Libra blue; Scorpio dull brown; Sagittarius yellow; Capricorn black; Aquarius sky blue; Pisces buff flecked silver-white. The author writes of the zodiacal colours of this scale: "it will be best to take this part of the column for what it may be worth as an uninitiated tradition. I am myself unable to attach any serious and important meaning to the majority of the symbols." (read, partly)
- Elements: "Scarlet naturally represents the activity of Fire, blue the passivity of Water, while yellow is the balance between them. Green is the middle colour of the spectrum"; earth has "citrine, olive, russet, and black" in the Queen scale. (read)

##### Robert Hand, Horoscope Symbols (1981)

Astrology: yes.

Edition opened: scanned text at https://archive.org/download/robert-hand-horoscope-symbols-schiffer-publishing-1981/Robert%20Hand%20-%20Horoscope%20Symbols-Schiffer%20Publishing%20%281981%29_djvu.txt

- Of the Moon: "The glyph ... of the Moon is a bowl on edge, facing either left or right (depending on whether one represents the Moon as waxing or waning). The glyph is obviously derived from the crescent-shaped form the Moon takes ... The bowl shape also suggests a container, which gives form to, limits, and helps define whatever it contains." Sun as yang, Moon as yin. (read)
- A search of the scan for "glyph" found only this planetary glyph passage plus uses of the sign glyphs for marking degrees; I found no passage in it assigning colours to planets. (read)

##### Dane Rudhyar

I did not open Rudhyar's The Astrology of Personality (1936); the archive copy is lending-only. I recall that it reads planets and signs through circle, crescent and cross (spirit, soul, body). (memory)

#### Section 4. Glyph origins and palmistry

##### Origin of the planetary glyphs (secondary sources)

- A summary of the standard account, from the Wikipedia article "Planetary symbols": the crescent has stood for the Moon "since antiquity"; Mercury's is a caduceus, with a cross later added; Venus's circle and cross "is conjectured to be a depiction of the hand-mirror of the goddess" (cited as not certain); the Sun's circle with centre dot "possibly represents Apollo's golden shield with a boss", the older form being "a circle with a single ray"; Mars's is "a shield and a spear"; Jupiter's "was originally a Greek zeta"; Saturn's "derives from the initial letters (Kappa, rho) of its ancient Greek name Κρόνος". These are the article's claims with its citations (Neugebauer, Maunder, Jones, Stearn); I did not check those works. (read: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Planetary_symbols, first 100,000 of about 165,000 characters; the section on the nodes lay beyond what I read)
- Deborah Houlding's study of the Greek horoscope L 497 says "Most of the planetary symbols are similar to our familiar, modern glyphs"; the Sun and Moon symbols are the only ones found in diagrams before the Byzantine period; the modern Sun glyph "does not appear to be in evidence before the Renaissance"; a Capricorn glyph in the chart "turned upside down ... would bear a close resemblance to that currently used for Saturn". (read as summarised by the page fetch, https://www.skyscript.co.uk/greek_horoscope.html; I did not read the whole article)
- Itten, in a passage on the circle: "The astrological symbol for the sun is a circle with a dot in the center." (read; see Itten below)

##### Palmistry mounts: Cheiro, Language of the Hand (1898)

Astrology: Cheiro disclaims it. Edition opened: https://archive.org/download/cheiroslanguageo00chei_1/cheiroslanguageo00chei_1_djvu.txt (chapter "The Mounts, their Position and their Meanings", pp. 63-65).

- Cheiro writes: "I do not use these names in any sense in relation to what is known as Astrological Palmistry. I do not for one moment deny that there may be a connection ... I use such names as Venus, Mars, Saturn, etc., simply as a quicker way of giving the student an idea of the qualities I wish to describe." (read)
- Positions: Mount of Jupiter "at the base of the first finger" (ambition, pride, desire for power); Saturn "at the base of the second finger" (solitude, prudence, "somber things"); Sun or Apollo "at the base of the third finger" (love of beauty); Mercury "at the base of the fourth finger" (wit, travel, change); two Mounts of Mars, the first "beneath the Mount of Jupiter, but inside the line of life" (active courage), the second between Mercury and Luna (passive courage); Luna "on the side of the hand beneath the Mount of Mars and directly opposite the Mount of Venus" (imagination). (read)
- Hand shapes are named without planets: square, spatulate, conic, psychic, mixed, with subdivisions by finger shape. (read, contents list)
- The line of fate is also titled "the Saturnian" ("The Line of Fate, the Line of Destiny, or the Saturnian"). (read)

#### Section 5. Colour, shape and feeling in design (no astrology, or astrology only in passing)

##### Goethe, Theory of Colours / Farbenlehre (1810)

Astrology: no. The text uses the word "mystical" in its last section and mentions the triangle, the hexagon and a "heavenly" generation, but names no planet.

Edition opened: Charles Eastlake's translation (1840), as the Project Gutenberg text 50572 mirrored at https://archive.org/download/goethestheoryofc50572gut/50572-0.txt. Section numbers are Goethe's; Part VI is "Effect of Colour with Reference to Moral Associations".

- §§764-777, the two sides: "The colours on the plus side are yellow, red-yellow (orange), yellow-red ... The feelings they excite are quick, lively, aspiring." "The colours on the minus side are blue, red-blue, and blue-red. They produce a restless, susceptible, anxious impression." (read)
- Yellow: "nearest the light ... a serene, gay, softly exciting character"; gold gives "a new and high idea of this colour"; but soiled yellow is "foul" and "the colour of honour and joy [is] reversed to that of ignominy and aversion", with the yellow hats of bankrupts and yellow circles of the Jews as examples. (read, §§765-771)
- Blue: "a principle of darkness"; "a blue surface seems to retire from us"; "an impression of cold"; blue rooms "appear in some degree larger, but at the same time empty and cold". Red: "an impression of gravity and dignity, and at the same time of grace and attractiveness"; it "includes all the other colours".
